[trialAccount t18280410-167]
959. HENRY GEORGE was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of March, 1 chaise-cart, value 5l., the goods of William Williams.
WILLIAM WILLIAMS. I live at Hornsey; on the 14th of March at night, I saw my cart in the coach-house, which was not locked - the gates were not on, as it was not finished; I missed it next morning.
JOHN VANN. I am a Bow-street patrol. On the 18th of March, I took the prisoner about one hundred yards from a stable at Hoxton, and asked if he had not got a cart to sell, he said, No, he did not deal in carts; I took him to the stable and broke the door open - I found the chaise-cart all taken to pieces - Mr. Williams saw it and owned it.
Cross-examined by MR. PRENDERGAST. Q. Did the prisoner tell you to whom it belonged? A. I heard him say he was employed to break it up, and I think he said, he did not know the man.
JOSEPH FRYER. I was with Vann, and asked the prisoner where he got the cart; he said he knew nothing about it, but when we got to the stable, he said he was employed to break it up by a man who had employed him once to move some goods, but he did not know where he lived.
JOSEPH KING. I am a wheelwright, and have repaired the cart; I know it to be Mr. Williams'.
THOMAS GLASSCOCK. I am a tailor, and have a stable at Hoxton. Two young men came to look at it about the 12th or 13th; the prisoner afterwards came with the person who took it; they called me up at one o'clock in the morning of the 15th; the other said his man could not get in, and the horse was getting cold; I got up, went down, and found the prisoner and the other man; the horse was out of the chaise. I went to take the irons off the gate, but the irons were gone; I forget the name of the man who took the stable - he appeared respectable, and referred me to a person at Shoreditch, but I did not inquire; I did not take notice of the chaise.
Cross-examined. Q. Was he not a superior looking man to the prisoner? A. Yes, very much so; his name was Brown, now I recollect.
ROBERT DRAKE. I am a baker, and live in Old-street. Brown applied to me. I think on Monday, the 18th of March, about selling a chaise cart - the prisoner came with him. I went to the stable in Huntingdon-street, Hoxton, to look at the cart, but did not like the appearance of it, and did not buy it - it was all to pieces - every bolt was taken out; he asked 5l. for it; I left the prisoner in the stable when I went away, and next evening he came to me with a message from Brown, saying he wished I would go and buy the cart, as they were very much in want of money - I cannot swear whether he said I or he.
Cross-examined. Q. He delivered Brown's message? A. I considered so. Brown was the only active man on the first occasion; I have known Brown for years, and all his family - he is very respectable, and highly connected.
Prisoner's Defence. I was employed by this man to break the cart up; he came to me on the 14th, I think, and asked me to go with him to put up the horse and chaise; I said I would, as I was up, and went; he called in the morning, and asked me to break it up - when I asked for my money he said he had none, but would pay me in a day or two.
ANN WINDSOR. I live at No. 40, Bath-street, City-road. I have known the prisoner two years; he lived in Baldwin-street. About one o'clock on the night of the 14th(I think) I was helping his wife to make a dress; I heard a horse and chaise stop, and some person called George - he went out, and returned in about half an hour.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280410-168]
960. THOMAS ROWLES was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of March, 1 handkerchief, value 3s., the goods of James Plucknett.
JAMES PLUCKNETT. I am an attorney, in Gray's Inn. On the 18th of March I was in Long-acre, I felt a touch at my pocket, and on turning missed my handkerchief; I saw nobody, and went on, but in a few minutes Roberts came up, and said, "Sir, you have lost your handkerchief;" I said, "Yes, but I suppose the thief is gone" - he said No, he was in the hands of an officer; I went back, and found the prisoner in the hands of Betraun.
THOMAS ROBERTS. On the 18th I saw the prisoner take the handkerchief from the prosecutor's pocket - there was another youth with him. I went and told the gentleman.
Prisoner. Sir Richard Birnie would not take that man's word; they took me to Marlborough-street - Roberts said"We must not take him to Bow-street." Witness. We went to Marlborough-street because Betraun had anothercharge there. I heard a paragraph read here yesterday, but I am not the person. (See page 466.)
ANGELIOUS BETRAUN. I was with Roberts, and saw the prisoner with another lad; the prisoner left him about two yards behind, and went up to the prosecutor - he suddenly turned away from him, and ran; I pursued, and caught him - he threw the handkerchief down; I took it up, and Roberts told the prosecutor, who claimed it; I did not say."We must not go to Bow-street," nor did Roberts - I took him first to the watch-house, and they would not take him in, because it was not eight o'clock. I took him to Bow-street - it was then just eight, and the door was shut; I took him to the watch-house again, and next morning having to go to Marlborough-street on another charge, I took him there, as I could not be at two places.
Prisoner's Defence. I saw Betraun running after another boy; he turned, and took me.
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Recommended to Mercy. - Whipped and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18280410-169]
961. JOHN BRISTOW was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of March, 1 piece of wood, value 9s., the goods of Ann Aldridge,
ALFRED WESTON. I am in the employ of Ann Aldridge, timber-merchant, of Bunhill-row. On the 7th of March I was in the yard between twelve and one o'clock; I saw the prisoner loitering about the gate for half an hour; I watched him, and saw him take this plank of Spanish mahogany; he took it outside, set it down, and ran down Bunhill-row; our porter followed him, and I fetched the plank in.
STEPHEN BOWER. I am the porter. I saw the prisoner lurking about the door; he came and took the wood out; I ran after him, and fetched him back.
JOHN BROWN. I am an officer, and saw the prisoner running.
Prisoner's Defence. I could not lift that wood off the ground; I was running to see Punch and Judy when I was taken.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 14.
Whipped, and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18280410-170]
962. WILLIAM DRAPER was indicted for stealing, on the 15th of March, 1 watch, value 2l.; 1 seal, value 7s.; 1 key, value 7s., and 1 watch-chain, value 6d, the goods of Charles Hopewell, from his person.
CHARLES HOPEWELL. On the 5th of March, I was in White Horse-street, Stepney, at near eleven o'clock that night; when I was opposite the watch-house, I met the prisoner coming towards me, and made way for him to pass on the left-hand side against the wall; he took hold of my watch-chain; I collared him - a scuffle ensued, and we both fell; he was uppermost, and when we were down he said, "Let me go," or something to that effect: I said,"No; if you go, you shall take me with you" - I called, Watch! the two headboroughs came, and took us both into the watch-house - I gave charge of him; they searched him, and could not find the watch; there was no one else near me - I am sure he was the person.
Cross-examined by MR. CLARKSON. Q. Were you quite sober? A. I had been drinking, but had not lost my senses - I had two glasses of gin and water - my watch was brought into the watch-house without the case; I had not seen him throw it away; I saw nobody else near me.
JOHN GROOM. I am a headborough. I saw the prisoner on the ground, on the top of the prosecutor; when they got up, the prosecutor said, "I charge this man with taking my watch; take him into custody:" the prisoner said nothing - I took him to the watch-house. Clark found the watch about six yards from where I took the prisoner.
Cross-examined. Q. Was he sober? A. He was neither drunk nor sober - he gave a full description of the watch.
ABRAHAM CLARK. I was with Groom; we heard a noise in the street; ran out of the watch-house, and saw the prosecutor flat on his back, and the prisoner on him - I took hold of the prisoner - Groom helped the prosecutor up; he was without his hat - I went out with a candle, and found the watch in the same line as I had brought the prisoner - the prosecutor described it - the case has not been found.
Cross-examined. Q. Did not the prisoner say he knew nothing about the watch? A. No, he never said a word.(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Confined One Year.
[trialAccount t18280410-171]
953. WILLIAM BASS and HANNAH BASS were indicted for stealing, on the 3d of March, 2 sacks, value 1s. 6d., and 15 lbs. weight of potatoes, value 6d., the goods of William Collins.
WILLIAM COLLINS. I live in Baltic-street. The prisoners, who are husband and wife, lodged with me - I deal in coals and potatoes. On the 3d of March I was going into my cellar, I told my boy to light a candle, and go down first; he did so, and found Hannah Bass in the cellar; he told me, and I took hold of her as she came up the stairs, and said, "This is the way you do it." She said, "What?" She then went into their room, which is on the ground floor; I went in with her, and saw a sack of mine on the floor; I took it up, and saw the boards were broken, and there was a hole down into the cellar; I then looked under their bed, and saw two sacks belonging to a man whom I deal with, and a quantity of potatoes, some kidney and some other - another lodged of mine and William Bass were sitting in the room, and I said, "This is a pretty start:" the other woman said, "What?" I said, "Mrs. Bass was in my cellar" - she clapped her hands together, and said, "I dare say she went there to hang herself" - the man said he knew nothing about it - I sent them all to the watch-house.
HENRY WIDDERSON. I live with the prosecutor; I went into the cellar about three o'clock that afternoon; I saw Mrs. Bass in the corner of the cellar - I came up and said, "Uncle there is Mrs. Bass in the cellar;" she came up - he laid hold of her - there was a board wrenched up in their room, and a hole down into the cellar.
ROBERT LOCK. I took the prisoner - there had been a square hole cut in the floor of the room, but the boards had been fixed down again, and required force to get them up.
Hannah Bass put in a written defence, stating, that being in great distress, and her family in a state of starvation, she had taken a few potatoes, but that the sacks were only used as a covering instead of blankets.
COURT to WILLIAM COLLINS. Q. Have they a family? Yes? I found the two sacks under the bed, by the side of a pail full of potatoes; they were very badly off; she is a drinking character, but I believe he is as worthy man as ever lived.
WILLIAM BASS'S Defence. I have worked many years for a japanner, of the firm of Howard and Co - poverty and distress have overtaken me - and, from the failure of my sight, and the state of my hand, which is disabled, I was discharged from my employ.
WILLIAM BASS - NOT GUILTY.
HANNAH BASS - GUILTY. Aged 42.
Recommended to Mercy by the Jury. Confined 1 month.
[trialAccount t18280410-172]
964. HENRY PIERCY GARNSEY was indicted or stealing, on the 28th of March, 1 pair of scissors, value 2s., the goods of Henry Shepherd.
HENRY SHEPHERD. I am a journeyman hair-dresser, and live in Kingsland-road. I lost a pair of scissors on the 28th of March; I had been using them about ten minutes before; I did not see them taken; I know them by a mark on the bow; I never saw the prisoner in the shop.
EDWARD OXLEY. I am a watchman. On the 28th of March, between ten and eleven o'clock at night, I was in Kingsland-road; I saw the prisoner skulking about shops, and peeping in at doors; I told him to go on, which he refused; I took him in charge, and the officer found these scissors on him.
JOHN ROBINSON. I live in Kingsland-road. I saw the prisoner about the road; Oxley took him, and found the scissors on him.
BARNARD JEFFERY. I am an officer; between ten and eleven o'clock that night I was coming up Union-street, and heard the prisoner abusing the watchman; I told him to go along; he said he was a gentleman; I took him, and found these scissors on him.
Prisoner's Defence. I have been to the shop two or three years to be shaved; I can be upon oath that I bought the scissors two years ago, and paid for them; I was waiting for a person who was not in the way.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280410-173]
965. ELIZA COLLINS was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of March, 1 coat, value 1l.; 2 gowns, value 7s.; 1 shawl, value 10s.; 1 bonnet, value 10s.; 1 handkerchief, value 1s.; and 2 shirts, value 4s., the goods of John Fellingham.
ELIZA FELLINGHAM. I am wife of John Fellingham, carpenter, of No. 7, Lambert's-grove. The prisoner was my servant. On the 18th of March I went out at seven o'clock in the morning; there was a chest of drawers in my room, which I locked; I returned at twelve o'clock at noon, but did not notice any thing then; I went out, and returned about seven o'clock in the evening; I then saw the key in another drawer; I had left it in the top drawer; I opened the drawer, and missed the articles stated, which are my husband's property. I found the prisoner between nine and ten o'clock that evening; she had this gown and shawl, and this Leghorn bonnet on; and she told the officer where the duplicate of the coat and other things were.
WILLIAM HENRY TAYLOR. I am a pawnbroker; I have a coat pawned, on the 18th of March, by the prisoner, in the name of Sarah Fellingham.
DICKENSON SOWERBY. I am a pawnbroker. I have two shirts pawned by the prisoner on the 18th of March.
RICHARD BETTY. I took up the prisoner on the night of the 18th of March; I found these articles on her.
BENJAMIN REEVES. I am an Officer. I received the prisoner in charge; she told me where she had put the duplicates, and I found them.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280410-174]
966. RICHARD DOWNES was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of April, 1 carpet, value 2l., the goods of John Packer.
JOHN PACKER. I am a broker, and live in Long-alley. On the 5th of April I saw the prisoner in my shop; his father lives just by; I saw him soon after running away with my carpet; he was pursued and dropped it.
ROBERT SELL. I live opposite the prosecutor; I saw the prisoner, about ten o'clock that morning with the carpet under his arm, in a very loose way. I suspected he did not come honestly by it, and told the prosecutor, who pursued and took him.
THOMAS SAPWELL. I am a constable. The prosecutor was pursuing the prisoner; I stopped him, and saw this carpet drop.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. - I had been after a place, and was running home, when this gentleman caught hold of me.
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18280410-175]
967. ABBY DESMOND was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of March, 2 coats, value 7s.; 2 aprons, value 1s., and 3 flat-irons, value 1s., the goods of Thomas Dilzon.
THOMAS DILZON. I live at Mr. Grove's, in Queen-street, Seven-dials, and the prisoner lived there also. I left this property in the care of Mrs. Groves, about four o'clock on the 29th of March, when I went out, and when I came home next morning, they were gone - I never gave them to the prisoner.
MARY GROVES. The prosecutor and the prisoner lodged in my house. Dilzon gave these things into my care, and I hung the coats upon the rail of my bedstead - the prisoner was helping me to iron that day; I went out at eight o'clock to take some things home, and when I came back she and the articles were gone - she had been with me a few days.
MAURICE NICHOLAS. I took the prisoner up in High-street, St. Giles'; there was a young woman with her, who had a coat in her apron; I asked the prisoner where the other coat was; she said in her box, at No. 25, Buckeridge-street - I went there, and found it, and these two aprons.
ANN PIGGOTT. I went with the prisoner to Mr. Jones', the pawnbroker, to pawn three flat-irons and a handkerchief - I pawned them in my own name, and gave the money and the duplicates to her.
JOSEPH HALL. I am a pawnbroker. I took in these articles - I saw Piggott, but not the prisoner.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. He told me he was going to leavethat Saturday night, and told me to take these things, and put into my box; I thought they had a better right to be there than in Mrs. Grove's room: we had lived together for three months - he made me leave my service, and come and live with him.
MARY GROVES re-examined. They did not live together; the prosecutor is a baker, and used to sleep a few hours, and then go to work - the prisoner had left her service, and I took her in out of charity.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280410-176]
968. BENJAMIN GUILLIS was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of February, 2 coats, value 30s., and 1 hat, value 5s., the goods of William Walter.
WILLIAM WALTER. I lost two coats, and a hat on the 18th of February, from my kitchen, in Earl-street. Kensington; my wife told me they were gone - I had seen one coat safe on the day before, which was Sunday.
ANN WALTER. I am the wife of William Walter. The prisoner had a coat lent him three weeks ago, to go to his father's, but he brought it back again; he told me on the Sunday he was going to his father's, and asked for the coat; I said my husband would be angry, and I would not lend it him; I went into the back kitchen, and he took it off a nail; when I returned into the kitchen, the prisoner, the coat, and the hat, were gone - there was no one else in the house but my little girl.
WILLIAM BENNETT. I am a constable. I met the prisoner about a fortnight afterwards in High-street; he came up to me, and surrendered himself; he said, "Have you not got a warrant against me?" I said, "A warrant against you! No;" he said, "My name is Guillis:" I said, "You are my prisoner;" he had this old coat and this hat on - he said he had sold the other coat.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I have always been in the habit of wearing these things.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18280410-177]
969. MARY HAGAN was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of March, 1 shift, value 1s., and 1 frock, value 1s., the goods of John Barand.
NANCY BARAND. I am the wife of John Barand; we live in Little Earl-street, Seven-dials. I was in my parlour on the evening of the 6th of March; I heard a noise, went out, and found the prisoner at the foot of the stairs - I had known her some years; these things laid at her feet - they had been taken off a line; I asked what possessed her to take them; she said if I would forgive her, she would never do the like again, and fell on her knees.
JOHN GREEN. I am an officer, and took the prisoner.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I went there to inquire for a person, who I was told lived there, and as I came down stairs this lady came out - she brought down these things, and said I had taken them off the bannisters.
GUILTY. Aged 34.
Confined Three Weeks.
[trialAccount t18280410-178]
970. CAROLINE MEYERS was indicted for stealing on the 11th of March, 1 sheet, value 3s., the goods of Robert Bryant.
MARY BRYANT. I am wife of Robert Bryant, we live in Ironmonger-row, St. Lukes. On the 10th of March the prisoner came and left her box at our house and went out - she came again next day to lodge there; she went out at eight o'clock that night, very quick - I went to her room and missed a sheet and pillow-case; she returned in half an hour with a basket, I sent for the watchman, and asked her for the sheet, she said, "You need not bother about a trumpery sheet, I'll get you another tomorrow."
WALTER CASTELLO. I am a watchman. I took the prisoner - who said, she was in distress; I found the duplicate on her, and got it out.(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 40.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Three Weeks.
[trialAccount t18280410-179]
Before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
971. EDWARD BAKER alias EDWARD REILY , was indicted for stealing on the 26th of February, 269 lbs. weight of lead, value 44s., the goods of, and belonging to George Dawson and Francis Child, and fixed to a certain building of theirs.
GEORGE DAWSON. I am in partnership with Francis Child, we are wine-merchants, and have several houses at Islington; our servant is in possession of them for us.
JOHN GARDENER. I am a milkman, and live in George-place, Holloway. I keep my cows in a field adjoining Mr. Dawson's premises - I went there about seven o'clock in the evening, on the 20th of February, and heard something on the houses; I went up to the cows - returned, and heard the noise again; it appeared to be on the slates of the house - I heard it several times, and at nine o'clock I spoke to the watchman - we went to the houses; I stopped in front, while he went round - the prisoner and another person came down from inside one of the houses - the other got away; I followed the prisoner for near a mile, and near the New North Bridge; I called for him to be stopped, and he was stopped - I had never lost sight of him; I am certain he ran from the house where I heard the knocking - I went on the roof after he was secured and found five pieces of lead weighing more than 200 lbs. rolled up, it was quite fresh cut.
ROBERT ALLEN. I am a watchman. On this night I saw the prisoner running without his shoes, pursued by Gardener - I stopped him, and took him to the watch-house.
WILLIAM FLANNAGAN. I am a watchman. Gardener came to me - I went to the house with him, and heard somebody on the roof; I saw the prisoner and another person come down. they both ran away from the back of the house - we followed; and the prisoner was secured - I was not near enough to swear to his face; I went on the roof by a ladder - there were no stairs; I found five rolls of lead which I saw matched, it corresponded exactly.
GEORGE HARPER. I found a pair of high shoes, about twenty yards from the back of the premises; they fit the prisoner - there is a bunnion on his right foot, which corresponds with a mark on the right shoe.
Prisoner's Defence. I had been to Barnet; I fellsleep, and my shoes were taken off my feet at the foot(Highgate-hill; I got up walked on, and was taken.
GEORGE HARPER. The prisoner gave this letter to a person to put into the post, it was stopped. - (read.)
To Mrs. Payne, at Mr. Dixons, Blacksmith, James-street, Bethnal Green.
DEAR SARAH, - I am sorry to inform you I am in trouble, in a charge of stealing lead, and am at Hatton-Garden; if you will go to Mr. Hardens and inform them, and ask them if they have got a pair of his old ones to lend me. I shall be at Newgate or Clerkenwell; I hope you will not take it to eart; do not forget Edward Riley; I have gone by the ame of Edward Baker.'
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280410-180]
972. GEORGE BROWN and ISAAC CLAPHAM were indicted for stealing, on the 1st of March, 1 handkerchief, value 5s., the goods of William Henry Deedes, from his person.
CLAPHAM pleaded GUILTY. Aged 20.
Transported for Life.
MR. WILLIAM HENRY DEEDES. I am a Government officer at the board of Commissioners. On the 1st of March, at three o'clock I was opposite St. James' Church, Piccadilly; I had a handkerchief in my coat pocket - I did not miss it till the officer produced it; the prisoner was then about two yards from me - there was rather a crowd as the Welch children were going to church.
THOMAS THOMPSON. I am an officer. On the 1st of March, about half-past two o'clock, I was in Piccadilly, and saw the prisoner attempting several people's pockets; I saw them close on the prisoner - Clapham took something from his pocket; I seized him, and he dropped it immediately - Brown had covered him; I had followed them from Gray's-Inn-lane with the procession.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Do you mean to say you saw Brown touch any pocket? A. No.
DANIEL REARDON. I am an officer. I was with Thompson and saw the prisoners together - Clapham tried several gentlemen's pockets; the prisoner covered him - they were in company together; as the prosecutor came along they closed upon him - Clapham took the handkerchief and was handing it to the prisoner, we took them both.
Cross-examined. Q. Had Brown touched it at all? A. No; I have made enquiry about him, he is not known to the officers at all.(Property produced and sworn to.)
BROWN'S Defence. I was going on an errand; I saw the procession and got up by the church - the constables told the mob to stand back, and they took me.
BROWN - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280410-181]
973. SIDWELL HALEY was indicted for stealing, on the 31st of March, 1 bag, value 2d.; 1 half-crown, and 2 penny-pieces, the property of Elizabeth Wickmill, from the person of Jane Brown .
JANE BROWN. I am fourteen years old, and live with my mother. On the 31st of March, at seven o'clock in the evening, I was going along with my cousins; the prisoner and three other girls, came up to us; the prisoner left the others, came up, and asked us for a penny; I had none - my cousin had a halfpenny, and I had one; we put them together and gave them to her. I had never seen her before; she then asked for another penny, I said I had no more of our own: she said she knew we had, and that it was to make up three quarterns of gin, and we should have some. I said we never drank gin; she said she would never let us go clear, if we did not give it to her - she put her hand down my bosom and tore the beads off my neck; she threw them at me, and said, "You may have them again." I had a bag of money down my bosom, I took it out, and held it tight in my hand - she took my hand, forced it open, and pulled it out; my cousin asked her for the money, she took and hit her in the eye, and gave her a dreadful blow - she began to run; I called Stop thief! and she was taken; she gave my bag and money to another girl. I am sure she is the girl who took it - there were two half-crowns and two penny pieces in it; they belonged to Elizabeth Wickmill.
ELIZABETH BROWN. I am fifteen years old. I was with Jane Brown, and in Duke-street, Manchester-square, I saw the prisoner come and take the purse; I asked her to give it up - she struck me in the eye and ran away; my cousin called Stop thief! nobody stopped her - I then called out and she was stopped. She gave the money to another girl, who got away.
GEORGE PONDER. I saw the crowd, and took the prisoner in charge.
Prisoner. I leave it to the mercy of the Court.
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18280410-182]
974. JAMES MATTHEWS was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of March, 1 handkerchief, value 4s., the goods of Robert John Parker, from his person.
ROBERT JOHN PARKER. I am a clerk. On the 17th of March, I was in Museum-street, at nine o'clock in the evening, I left a jerk at my pocket, and missed my handkerchief; I saw the prisoner close to me, and accused him of it - he denied it: I called the watchman, and he ran away - I cried Stop thief! and he was taken, without my losing sight of him.
Cross-examined by Mr. CLARKSON. Q. Before he ran away, did he not offer to be searched? A. No, it was dark, the lamps were lighted.
DANIEL HONE. I live in Castle-street. I was in my shop, and heard a cry of Stop thief! I looked out, and saw the prisoner passing; I went after him, and two men stopped him - he dragged them across the street, to Mr. Yardley's area, and laid down; I then took him to a light, where he was searched - but nothing found on him; he struggled very much - we found the handkerchief in the area.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you lose sight of him? A. No; he was taken five yards from my door - I am a constable, and took him to the watch-house.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I had been on an errand.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18280410-183]
975. JAMES JOHNSON was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of February, 1 watch, value 4l., the goods of John Cargill.
JOHN CARGILL. I am a mariner, and belong to the Truewhich lay off Hermitage-stairs; the prisoner was engaged on board that vessel. On the 28th of February, I sent him on shore with my things to wash; he did not return - I went down and missed my watch from a drawer in my cabin; I had put it there about eight o'clock that morning; he was found at the Crooked Billet, public-house, with two women.
GEORGE DEVERELL. I am an officer, and took him in charge. I heard two women had been with him at the Crooked Billet - I went there and took them; the duplicate of the watch was found there, under the seat where he had been sitting.
ROBERT LINWOOD. I am a pawnbroker, and live in Ratcliff-highway; the prisoner pawned this watch, on the 28th of February.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I met a young man, and we went to have some beer. I heard the Theatre had fallen down. went to look at it; then went to another house, and was taken.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.

976. ANN McINTYRE was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of February, 1 pair of sheets, value 8s.; 1 blanket, value 3s.; 1 bolster, value 3s.; 1 pillow, value 2s.; 1 looking-glass, value 2s., and 1 candlestick, value 1s. 6d., the goods of Samuel Howard.
SAMUEL HOWARD. I live in Leg-alley, Long-acre. On the 3d of February, the prisoner took my second floor front room, at 5s. a week; she left me in ten days without notice, and I missed this property - I found her on the 25th; she said she had pawned my things and intended to redeem them, when she got the money - she told us where they were pawned; we went, and found they had been redeemed - I have not recovered them.
WILLIAM OXLEY. I am a constable. On the 25th of February, I took the prisoner, and found on her three duplicates, not relating to this property; she told me where she had pawned the things, but they had been taken out.
PHILIP EATON. I am a pawnbroker, and live in High Holborn. The prisoner pawned a sheet on the 5th of February, it was redeemed on the 15th.
CHARLES WILLIAMS. I am a pawnbroker. The prisoner pawned a pillow on the 5th of February, and redeemed it by an affidavit, on the 20th.
The prisoner put in a written defence, pleading distress.
GUILTY. Aged 40.
Transported for Seven Years.

977. GEORGE TURNER was indicted for stealing, on the 2d of April, 1 rule, value 1s., and 1 saw, value 3s., the goods of Edward Hill.
EDWARD HILL. I am a carpenter. On the 2d of April, I was at work in the North-road, Islington; I went to dinner at twelve o'clock, leaving my tools in the front parlour; the house was not finished - when I returned I missed these tools; the prisoner is a stranger.
THOMAS KIRKWOOD. I am a pawnbroker, and live in Brick-lane, Old-street. I have a saw, pawned on the 2d of April, by the prisoner. I believe; but am not quite certain - this is the duplicate I gave for it.
JAMES TAYLOR. I am a constable, and took the prisoner about four o'clock, on the 2d of April, and found this duplicate of the saw on him.(Saw produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I met a young man, who asked me to pawn the saw.
GUILTY. Aged 16.

978. JOHN TAYLOR was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of February, 1 round frock, value 5s.; 2 pairs of stockings, value 2s., and 1 shirt, value 2s., the goods of Elijah Ventham.
ELIJAH VENTHAM. I live at Hounslow. I lost these things on the 26th of February, out of my box, which was in the loft, at Mr. Woodwards, where I live; it was about seven o'clock - I was feeding a team of horses at the door, and heard a noise in the left; I went and looked up, and saw the prisoner coming down; I asked what he did there, he said he went there to lay down - I asked what he had got under his frock; he said, his shirt - I took it out, and it was this property of mine; he was a stranger - he struggled, but I called for help, and he was secured.
WILLIAM KEEN. I live at Hounslow. Ventham called for assistance; I went up and took the prisoner, and saw these articles taken from him.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I had come out of the country; a man stood by the horse-trough - I asked him where I could lay down, as I was in distress; he took me up to the loft, gave me the things out of the box, and said if I took them to Hounslow, they would tell me where to find him - and he would give me 1s.
GUILTY. Aged 29.
Transported for Seven Years.

WILLIAM BACON. I live in Portpool-lane. On Sunday evening, the 23d of March, between seven and eight o'clock, I had been serving in my shop; I went out of the shop door to the private door, and met the prisoner coming out of the passage; I took hold of her, and asked what she had got; she dropped a plate which broke, and said, "It is from real hunger" - I took her back, and found these other articles in her pocket; they had been taken from my washhouse - she threatened to destroy herself.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 59.

981. DAVID DAVIS was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of February, 1 watch, value 1l., 1 seal, value 5s.; 1 watch-key, value 3s., and 1 ring, value 2s., the goods of Michael Dibly, from his person.
MICHAEL DIBLY. I work for Messrs. Stringer and Co. On the 10th of February, about ten o'clock at night, I was at the Three Crows public-house, City Road; I cannot say whether the prisoner was there: I had been there about two hours; six or seven people were in the taproom; I pulled out my watch about eight o'clock, and Eliza Morris, who was there, wanted to look at it; she took it, and in a few minutes I enquired for it; she said she had given it me back; she afterwards went out - I went out and gave her in charge - I had not noticed what she did with it: she was tried here last Session; I received a blow from Cooper, who has been tried.
JOHN KETT. I belong to the Guards. I was at the house, and saw Dibley, Morris, and Harding there; Cooper was not there - the prisoner sat next to Morris, who had the watch some time in her hand, and then laid it down by the prisoner, who took it up and gave it to Harding; he put it into his bosom, and went out; Davis followed; Dibley then went to the bar for an officer to take the girl; the prisoner was taken to the watch-house; I am sure he is the man who had the watch.
Prisoner. Q. Did you see me in the room before ten o'clock? A. Yes: I had come from Ware that day.
GEORGE WILMOT. I was at the watch-house when the complaint was made - I knew the prisoner, and on the Tuesday following I went and took him; I told him it was for a watch; he said, "I may thank Cooper for this" - he afterward said, "I admit I was in the room; but I had nothing to do with the watch" - the watch was returned to the prosecutor at the office by some girls.
THOMAS STOCKS. I was with Wilmot - the prisoner was discharged, but we were ordered to take him again.
Prisoner's Defence. I was not in the tap-room, but in the parlour, and heard a scuffle; I went out, but knew nothing of it.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280410-190]
982. ROBERT DIXON was indicted for stealing, on the 2d of March, 1 half-crown, 1 shilling, and 2 farthings, the monies of James Squires, from his person.
JAMES SQUIRES. I live with a tailor in New Kent-road. On the 2d of March, between three and four o'clock; I was looking at the ruins of the Brunswick Theatre; there were a great many people there; I felt the prisoner's hand coming out of my waistcoat pocket, where I had this money; I caught hold of him, and asked him for my money: the officer got it from him. I lost a half-crown, a shilling, and two farthings: the shilling dropped; I took it up, and gave it to the officer.
HENRY HILLIER. I was at the ruins of the Theatre, and saw the prosecutor there; he accused the prisoner, who was close to him, of stealing his money - I saw the prisoner put his hand behind him, and put some money into his coat pocket; I could not see what but something dropped.
THOMAS JOSEPH WEST. I am an officer. I took the prisoner, and found a half-crown in his pocket.
JAMES GLEDBURY. I am an officer. I saw the prisoner attempt several pockets before he went up to Squires - I went up, seeing a scuffle - Squires picked up a shilling and a farthing, and gave them to me.
Prisoner's Defence. I had this half-crown and three halfpence in my pocket - this gentleman put his hand into his pocket, he let his money fall, and charged me with it.
GUILTY. Aged 25.
Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18280410-191]
983. JAMES MALONEY was indicted for embezzlement.
PETER GRAHAM. I am assistant to John Graham, of High Holborn. The prisoner was his porter, and had a yearly salary; he was authorised to receive money when he delivered goods; he never accounted for 2l. paid by Mr. Whitehouse; which he should have done when he returned, or at all events on the same day. When he returned he said Mr. Whitehouse had sent out for change, but fearing he might be detained too long he was to call again for it.
Cross-examined by MR. CHURCHILL. Q. Did he not say he had paid Welch? A. Yes; he said so the next morning.
HENRY HOLLAND. I am a clerk to Mr. Whitehouse, of Thavies Inn. I was out when the parcel was brought, but the prisoner called about ten the next morning, and I paid him 2l. on Mr. Graham's account.
JOHN BURRELL WELCH. I am cashier to Mr. Graham. The prisoner never paid me this money; it is my duty to receive it - when it was enquired about he said he had given it to me, but I am sure he did not.
Cross-examined. Q. How many errors have you made in the last year? A. I cannot say; I have made a great many - the prisoner left in consequence of this accusation - he came back next morning, saying he was uneasy about it, and desired to have it investigated - he went before the Magistrate.
JOSEPH HEALEY. I am in Mr. Graham's employ; it is my duty to receive money in the absence of the cashier; the prisoner never paid me this 2l.
WILLIAM HOTSON. I am a clerk to Mr. Graham; the cashier accounts to me every evening, but never gave me this 2l. - the prisoner wished this to be fully investigated.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280410-192]

The prosecutor did not appear. NOT GUILTY.

985. WILLIAM ATTERSLEY was indicted for embezzlement.
GEORGE AVELING. I am an oilman, and live in Gloucester-street, Queen-square. The prisoner was my porter and received money for me; Mr. Harrison is a customer of mine; he lives in Spring-garden-terrace.
CATHERINE ROBERTS. I live with Mr. Harrison; the prisoner used to bring goods from Mr. Aveling; I paid him 19s. 8d. on the 12th of March, on account of his master, and he gave me this receipt. (read.)
MR. AVELING. He should have given me the money directly he came home; I never received this - Mr. Harrison pays his bills every Wednesday, and as it had stood over two or three weeks, I said to the prisoner, "How is it, Mr. Harrison has not paid his bill?" he said he was out of town - I am certain he never paid me the money.
THOMAS BENTLEY. I am shopman to Mr. Aveling.On the 17th of March I told the prisoner I was going to call on Mr. Harrison, as master was not satisfied about it: he then said he had received the bills, and what should he do about it.
JOHN LIMBRICK. I took him into custody, and found 21s. 6d. on him.
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that he did not intend to defraud his master of the amount
GUILTY. Aged 32.
Transported for Seven Years.

986. WILLIAM BELFORD was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of March, 2 lbs. of tobacco, value 7s., the goods of Joseph Ward.
BENJAMIN LORD. I am a Bow-street patrol. On the 26th of March, at a quarter-past seven in the evening, I met the prisoner between the Elephant and Castle and Newington Church; he had something in his jacket; I thought it was wrong, and looked at him; he set off running, and threw down this pracel - I pursued and took him; Shepherd took up the parcel of tobacco, and by the permit we found the owner.
JOHN SHEPHERD. I was with Lord - his evidence is correct; I picked up the parcel.
JOSEPH WARD. I keep a shop at Tottenham, Middlesex. This tobacco is mine, and was lost from my counter; I did not miss it till the officers brought it; I should think from the manner I was engaged that it was taken about six o'clock in the evening. GUILTY. Aged 17.

989. MATTHEW PLOMER was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of March, 1 stew-pan, value 7s., the goods of William Taylor Bell.
JAMES TERRY. I am a patrol. I stopped the prisoner in Cow-cross on the 5th of March, with this stew-pan under his arm, in a cloth; he said he had bought it for 2s. in Spitalfields.
RICHARD MILLEN. I was at the watch-house when the prisoner was brought in; he said he bought the pan in Crispin-street, for 2s. - I detained him; he then said a boy gave it to him.
WILLIAM TAYLOR BELL. I keep the Admiral Benbow, public-house, in Golden-lane. The prisoner used to come to the tap, and talk with my boy - this pan is mine; he said at the office that my pot-boy had given it to him: my boy absconded directly the officer came to the house; the pan was in my kitchen. I lost the key of the kitchen three or four days before.
Prisoner's Defence. I was in the house - this boy asked me to go and sell an old pan for him; he gave it to me; I was to sell it for old copper, and he was to give me what he thought proper.
GUILTY. Aged 42.
Confined Three Months.

990. JOHN GIBSON was indicted for stealing, on the 23d of March, 1 oak-joint, value 2s., the goods of Joseph Williamson and John Simmons.
JOSEPH WILLIAMSON. I am a carpenter, in partnership with John Simmons. On the 24th of March Franks showed me a plank, which had been taken from our yard in Hackney-road. I find the prisoner bears a very good character- I believe it is his first offence.
WILLIAM FRANKS. I am a watchman. On the 23d of March, about two o'clock in the morning, I stopped the prisoner in Wellington-square, about three hundred yards from the prosecutor's, with this plank on his shoulder - he said it was all right; he dropped it, and ran away: he was taken on the Thursday following; I knew him before.
THOMAS WEST. I took the prisoner.
The prisoner received an excellent character.
GUILTY. Aged 21.

[trialAccount t18280410-199]
991. WILLIAM MASON, JAMES LEGERWOOD, and WILLIAM HARRIS were indicted for stealing, on the 11th of March, 1 counterpane, value 5s., the goods of William Lewis.
WILLIAM LEWIS. I am a gardener, and live at Hammersmith. I saw this counterpane safe between eight and nine o'clock on the 11th of March, when I came home to breakfast, and in the evening it was gone; the prisoners all live at Hammersmith, about a mile from me.
ELIZABETH LEWIS. I saw the counterpane safe at one o'clock, when I had taken my husband's dinner - I did not see it again till Davie and I found it hanging up at Bolton's, on the 13th of March.
HANNAH WEAVING. I saw the counterpane safe between two and three o'clock in the afternoon, hanging up.
SARAH MILWOOD. I live at Hammersmith. About eleven o'clock on the morning the counterpane was stolen, I saw the three prisoners pass our house, towards where this counterpane was, and at four o'clock I saw them again- Mason separated from them, and went towards the back of Lewis' premises, which is about one hundred yards from our house.
ANN MILLWOOD. I saw the prisoners pass in the morning, and return in the afternoon.
ROBERT CHAMBERS, I live at Fulham. On the 12th of March Mason came and asked if I dealt in old rags - I said Yes; he then said it was not rags, but a counterpane, and gave it up to me in my cart; I said I did not want anything of that kind; it was in a blue handkerchief, and I took it down to Bolton's, who said he would give 4s. for it, if the man came honestly by it - Mason had asked me 5s. for it; Bolton gave me 4s., and I gave it to Mason - I did not know I was doing wrong.
WILLIAM BOLTON. I bought the counterpane of Chambers, for 4s., and hung it at my bed-room window, as I thought it was damp.
DAVID DAVIE. I found the counterpane at Bolton's. - I took Mason and Legerwood that evening; and Harris surrendered.
WILLIAM HELPS. On the 12th of March, about half-past ten o'clock, I saw the three prisoners pass my house together; Legerwood had a bundle under his arm, tied in a yellow handkerchief.
MASON - GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Seven Years.
LEGERWOOD - NOT GUILTY.
HARRIS - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280410-200]
Fourth Middlesex Jury - Before Mr. Common Sergeant.
999. HARRIET LUFF was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of February, 2 candlesticks, value 2s.; 1 piece of lace, value 6d.; and 1 blanket, value 1s., the goods of James Bond.
MR. J. ALLEY conducted the prosecution.
ANN BOND. I am wife of James Bond, and live at Brentford. I employed the prisoner as a char-woman - she had access to every part of the house. In February last I lost this property - I received information. and went with a search-warrant to her room; we found all these articles there.
Cross-examined by MR. PRENDERGAST. Q. This lace is on a cap? A. Yes; the cap is not mine - I know the lace by the pattern - it had been on a cap of mine; here are two pieces on the cap; only one is mine - the candlesticks are broken in pieces: they were broken, but not as they are now; I found them in a basket, with some rags; I had two yards of this lace - I know the blanket by the pattern - she owes my husband some money; she said the broken brass was given her by my servant.
MR. J. ALLEY. Q. Is there any mark on the candlesticks? A. Here is a mark of some lead, where my husband tried to mend it; they were in pieces, but not broken - My sister had sent me the lace; it was exactly of this pattern.
JOSEPH DICKINSON. I am an officer. On the 24th of March I went to the prisoner's room with the prosecutrix and others; I searched for a quarter of an hour, and by the side of the bed I found these candlesticks, tied up, in a basket - I found also the blanket and cap.
Cross-examined. Q. Did not the prosecutrix say the lace on the cap was hers? A. Yes; she did not say only part of it was hers.
JAMES BOND. I missed these candlesticks, and know them to be mine, by a mark which I made on one of them by turning a tap; and I mended this one with lead.
The prisoner put in a written defence, stating that the candlesticks were given to her as old metal by the prosecutrix's servant.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280410-201]
993. HARRIET LUFF was again indicted, for stealing on the 24th of March, 3 table-cloths, value 20s.; 15 knives, value 3s.; 15 forks, value 3s.; and 3 towels, value 3s., the goods of John Surman.
ELIZABETH SURMAN. I am wife of John Surman - the prisoner was in my service in 1824, and was frequently at my house, once or twice a week in 1826; these articles are mine - I missed them in 1826, with a great many other things - I swear to them; there is my own writing on the table-cloth; and I know the knives and forks.
Cross-examined. Q. Has she been employed by you since 1826? A. No: she was at my house on the 24th of March, but not with my knowledge; I was present when part of the property was found in her room; some of the knives and forks were on the table - I know one knife by having broken it; I lost some of the knives in 1825.
JOSEPH DICKINSON. I found twenty-eight duplicates in the prisoner's room, and some of these articles.
JOHN BURFORD. I am a pawnbroker; I have a tablecloth and two towels pawned on the 17th of July last; they are marked in the prisoner's own name and her husband's, I am sure she is the person who pawned them; the prosecutrix said her name has been picked out, but there is no appearance of that; she then said she could swear to the hemming, but many females work in the same way.
Cross-examined. Q. Did she not claim a great many more things? A. Yes; I thought she would swear to all I had got.
MRS. SURMAN. Here is the place on the table-cloth where my husband's name was; it has been picked out; the name of Surman was marked in full - there were several other things at the pawnbroker's which I could have sworn to.
ELIZABETH HUGHES. I bought these six green-handled knives and forks of the prisoner about four years ago.
ANN BOND. I bought this table-cloth of the prisoner; it was marked B. H. L. - I never altered it.
MRS. SURMAN. I know this table-cloth; I sewed it instead of hemming it - I have another of the same pattern.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you have all these things in 1825? A. Not the small green-handled knives and forks: the prisoner came to me on the 24th of February. and left on the 1st of May, 1824; her sister then came to me and left in August, 1826, and the prisoner came to char for me that year; I never gave the sister anything; but I have given the prisoner some old bed-furniture, baby-linen, and other things; the mark has been picked out of this table-cloth; there was some of the marking cotton on it when I found it.
JURY. Q. When were the table-cloths marked? A. That I cannot say; the whole of the three were bought in 1825; the mark of the thread cannot be washed out: I missed them in 1825 - the prisoner's sister left me in disgrace in 1826: I should rather suspect her than the prisoner.
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that some ofthe things were given her by her sister, and others by the prosecutrix. NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280410-202]
994. WILLIAM WELCH was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of March, 1 handkerchief, value 2s., the goods of James Peachey.
WILLIAM HENRY BAYFIELD. I am shopman to James Peachey, pawnbroker, of Goswell-street. On the afternoon of the 18th of March I observed some person as I thought unpinning the things at the door: I went round and saw the prisoner walk away, and put something into his pocket; I pursued - he ran, and threw this handkerchief out of his pocket; I took him; the handkerchief was taken up and given to me; I only lost sight of him as he turned the corner.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GEORGE FULLER. I am beadle of Christ's Hospital; I saw the prisoner in a little street turning out of Old-street, and the witness pursuing him; he caught him, and just before he came up he threw down this handkerchief, which I took up.
THOMAS WATTS. I am a constable, and was sent for; I took the prisoner, and found two other handkerchiefs on him. GUILTY. Aged 18.

995. ELLEN RYAN and CATHERINE GRIMES were indicted for stealing, on the 10th of March, 1 blanket, value 2s.; 2 sheets, value 3s.; 1 rug, value 1s.; 1 bolster, value 2s., and 1 bed, value 5s., the goods of Andrew Parsons.
ANDREW PARSONS. On the night of the 10th of March, the watchman sent for me; I went and saw Grimes taking a bed away from an unoccupied house of mine in Maynard-street, St. Giles'; I took her to the watch-house, then came back, and met Ryan coming out of the house with the other things in a bundle; the watchman had hold of her - she threw the things down by the church, and said she would carry them no further; I had locked the house up about five o'clock that evening - they were safe then.
JOHN MURPHY. I am a watchman. On the 10th of March, I was going by the passage of this house and saw Grimes standing by the side of this bed; I asked what she had there; she said a bed - I asked who it belanged to; she said it must belong to the person of the house; we took it into Mr. Groat - he said it belonged to Parsons: I afterwards saw Ryan, she appeared in liquor when she got near the watch-house, but not stupidly drunk.
CHARLES BENNIS. I am a watchman. I took Ryan with a bundle containing a blanket, a rug, a pair of sheets, and a bolster.(Property produced and sworn to.)
RYAN'S Defence. I was in liquor; a woman took me up and gave me the things.
GRIMES' Defence. I was going down the stairs, and found the bed: I thought it belonged to Mr. Groat, and asked him - I know nothing of it - it lay at the stair foot.
RYAN - GUILTY. Aged 25.
GRIMES - GUILTY. Aged 30.
Transported for Seven Years.

996. GEORGE JACKSON was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of March, 5 loaves of bread, value 2s. 6d., the goods of Henry Harris.
HENRY HARRIS. I am a baker. On the 7th of March I was out, and saw the prisoner take five loaves from my barrow; he run down Guildford-street with them - I followed and took them from him.
HENRY GRAMMER. I am a constable. I took the prisoner and the loaves - he said before the Magistrate that he had not eaten for two days. GUILTY. Aged 17.

[trialAccount t18280410-205]
997. JAMES ELLIS, WILLIAM BOYDEN, and THOMAS WILLIAMS, were indicted for stealing, on the 29th of February, 1 caravan, value 15l., the goods of Charles Henry Buttler.
CHARLES HENRY BUTTLER. I am a stable-keeper, and live in Little Portman-street, Cavendish-square. I had a caravan in the street, in front of my house, on Friday, the 22d of February; I saw it there at twelve o'clock at night, and in the morning it was gone - I saw part of it on the 2d of March.
Prisoner ELLIS. Q. To what part of it do you swear to? A. To the shafts, the wheel-plate, and part of the side.
FRANCIS KEYS. I am an officer. On Friday, the 29th of February, I saw the three prisoners coming down Whitechapel in a cart: they went into a mews in Great Garden-street, Whitechapel; Ellis then got out, and the other two drove the cart into the stable; I waited about half an hour, then got Fowler - we went and found Ellis standing at the top of the Mews; we took him down to the stable, where we found the other two sitting on some straw; we handcuffed them all together; I had seen them before that day in the Mews, and knew they kept their cart there.
JAMES FOWLER. I am an officer. I went with Keys to the stable, and found a large quantity of pieces of vans and carts, in a loft over the stable; Ellis called them his premises; I found Mr. Buttler and shewed him some parts of what I found, and he claimed them; I have not brought them here - there was a cart load altogether.
Prisoner ELLIS. Q. What did he swear to? A. Some of the iron and the shafts; he described them to me before he saw them; he said there was a hook broken underneath, and it was so; I found part of the iron in the Mews and part at McDowell's.
JOSEPH HAMMOND. I was with the officers, and brought the things from the loft; what they have stated is correct.
REBECCA ELIZABETH McDOWELL. My father keeps a marine store shop in Fieldgate-street, Whitechapel. On the last week in February, Ellis brought some old iron in a cart; I bought a sweep-plate and some other cartiron of him - I asked his name, he said, Ellis, and that it was his own - I broke the iron up myself, and it was put in among the other iron, and in due time it was separated - I cannot point out any part which I bought of him, except the plate; the Magistrate sent an officer to see what we had bought; I showed him what we had, and he took some away.
Prisoner ELLIS. Q. On what day of the week did I bring it? A. I believe on a Wednesday; I do not know the day of the month.
WILLIAM HARRIS. I am an officer; I went to McDowell's, and found some iron - Mr. Buttler has seen and claimed it; but I have not brought it here.
Prisoner ELLIS. Q. What did you get there? A. A sweep-plate, two staves, and some loose iron; there were several tons of iron there.
HENRY MARSHALL. I am a tripeman; a person resembling Ellis hired a stable of me in Great Garden-street, that is the stable the officers searched - he took it in his own name.
CHARLES WORGAN. I am carman to Mr. Emery, of Gun-square; I met Ellis in Whitechapel - I knew him by the name of Bullen; he asked, where I was going? I said, I had nothing to do; he asked if I would go with him to sell some old iron, and he would give me some beer - I went with him to McDowell's, and took the iron; there was a round plate and some other iron.
Prisoner ELLIS. Q. What day was it? A. I believe it was Wednesday - it was the latter end of February; your mother went by the name of Bullen - I have been in your loft and saw a pair of shafts there, I think you said, you were going to build a van with them in the summer for yourself; that might be three or four weeks ago.
COURT. Q. How long was it before you went to the Magistrate? A. About a fortnight; I only judge of the time I went with him by master having nothing to do - it was towards the end of February.
Prisoner ELLIS. Q. Was not the iron sold on the Wednesday week before I was apprehended? A. I think so; I was examined on the 17th of March, and think it was on the Wednesday week before that.
MR. BUTTLER. I saw the shafts, they are mine, and so is the sweep-plate, and some pieces of the cart I can swear to; I know the shafts by some plates and some hooks on them - I had had the van about two years; I bought it - it has been mended once, and I also had done something to it myself; I had put some plates on the shaft, and there are several parts of my mending which I can swear to.
Prisoner ELLIS. Q. Did you ever take the sweepplate off? A. No, but I have looked at it many times.
ELLIS'S Defence. He is a false swearing man, it is not his iron; I dealt in iron, and sold some there before - I was first examined on the 29th of February, and then Worgan said it was the Wednesday week before.(See Fifth Day.)
ELLIS - GUILTY. Aged 24.
BOYDEN - NOT GUILTY.
WILLIAMS - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280410-206]
998. SARAH DAY was indicted for stealing, on the 3d of March, 1 oz. and 5 dwts. of silver, value 6s., the goods of Clement Cheese.
CLEMENT CHEESE. I am a silversmith, and live in Clerkenwell, the prisoner was in my service. On the 1st of March I found I was deficient in my stock, and on the 3d I was deficient again; I told her on the Sunday that I was deficient, and I would know who it was - on the Monday I told her I was short again, and I would see in her box; she said, she was never called a thief before - I said, if she had nothing of mine, she could not object to my seeing her box; she hesitated - I went up stairs with my errand-boy; she opened her box and took out a small paper parcel, which she tried to put into her pocket - the boy stopped her hand, and we found it was this piece of silver; I told the boy to empty out the box, and we found some more silver - she fell on her knees and begged forgiveness; here is an ancient coin among it, which I know well, and a piece of an ingot; she only lived eleven days with me, and came from the office in Holborn.(Property produced and sworn to.)
BENJAMIN DUKE. I am Mr. Cheese's errand-boy; I saw the silver found in the prisoner's box - she asked forgiveness.
WILLIAM BROWN EDWARDS. I am an officer, and took her in charge.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280410-207]
999. WILLIAM HATTON was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of March, 1 knife, value 9d.; 3 shillings; 1 six-pence; and 104 farthings, the property of Thomas James.
ANN JAMES. I am wife of Thomas James; he keeps a shop in Pitfield-street, Hoxton - On the 12th of March, I was in the parlour and heard money jingle, and presently I heard it again; I then looked towards the shop, and saw something black, which I thought was the dog; but presently I saw a head - I called my husband, who was in bed in the parlour - he went and took the prisoner in the shop. I had seen 3s. 6d. in the till shortly before - I found the till open. and the box containing the silver taken out - I found the money under a mat behind the counter; he had got a knife up his sleeve.
ESTHER BLATCHLEY. I was at work with the prosecutrix; I went into the shop, and served the prisoner with nuts; I opened the till then, and saw some silver and farthings in it - he went out - I went into the room and sat down - we afterwards heard money jink twice; we went out, and I saw the prisoner behind the counter; 3s. 6d. was found under the mat; the farthings lay at a little distance; nobody came into the shop after him; he is a sweep.
GEORGE WILMOT. I am an officer, and took the prisoner.
GUILTY. Aged 14.
Confined Three Months.
