1000. JOHN M'GINNIS was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of March, 1 plane, value 2s.; one oil-stone, value 2s.; and 1 chisel, value 1s. 6d., the goods of James Howting; 2 saws, value 7s.; 1 plane, value 3s.; and 1 hammer, value 1s., the goods of Nathaniel Raven; two saws, value 5s.; 1 stock and 6 bits, value 5s.; 1 plane, value 2s.; 1 chisel, value 6d.; 3 gouges, value 6d.; 2 files, value 6d.; 1 screw-driver, value 6d.; and 1 pair of dividers, value 1s. the goods of William James.
JAMES HOWTING. I am a carpenter. On the 27th of March I was working at the new buildings, at the London Docks; Raven and James were also working there. On the evening of the 26th I left my tools safe in the corner, next the water-side, and next morning I missed some tools; these are them.
NATHANIEL RAVEN. I was working at the Docks - I lost two saws, a plane, and other tools, which I left safe on the night of the 26th; I had locked them up in a place, but the lock was wrenched off - I know nothing of the prisoner.
WILLIAM JAMES. I was working at the Docks, andlost a quantity of tools, which I had left safe the night before; I have not found them all.
WILLIAM MITCHELL. I am a Bow-street patrol; I took the prisoner on the morning of the 29th, as he was turning into a pawnbroker's shop with a saw down his coat; I asked where he got it - he said he bought it of a man in Rosemary-lane; I took him to the Docks - James claimed the saw; he told me where his lodgings were: I went and found the other tools, stated in the indictment - I found forty-seven duplicates on him.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner. I picked up some tools, and bought the others.
GUILTY. Aged 32.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280410-209]
1001. JOHN DEVINE was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of March, 1 door, value 16s., the goods of Elizabeth Hall.
ELIZABETH HALL. I am a widow. On the 12th of March I lost this door from an unfinished house, which my husband was building before he died - I know it by some marks on it.
THOMAS TUCHIN. I am a grocer, and live in Whiteconduit-fields. On the 12th of March, the prisoner passed my shop-door towards the prosecutrix's house, and in about three minutes he came back with the door on his shoulder; two other men were with him; I overtook him with it - he said a gentleman had employed him to carry it to the Angel; the other two men went away when they saw me pursuing him - he said he did not know the gentleman.
THOMAS HUNT. I went with Tuchin; I saw the door was gone from the house - we pursued and took the prisoner with it - he said, a man, whom he never saw before, was to give him sixpence to carry it to the Angel.
EDWARD PALMER. I am a Bow-street patrol. I came up and took the prisoner.
Prisoner's Defence. I was out of employ, and accepted of this job; I did not know the door was stolen.
GUILTY. Aged 26.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18280410-210]
FOURTH DAY. MONDAY, APRIL 14.
First Middlesex Jury. - Before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
1002. GEORGE CULLEY and THOMAS FAGAN were indicted for stealing, on the 28th of February, 1 cask, value 8s., the goods of Thomas Harris.
MR. ALLEY conducted the prosecution.
ROBERT DUKE. I am an officer of Hatton-garden. On the 28th of February I was in company with Bassett and Lloyd, near the house of Linton, at Islington-green; he is a retailer of table-beer, what is called a pinman; I saw the two prisoners about six o'clock in the evening, come there, and Culley had a firkin on his shoulder; they were walking in company together; I should think they were a mile and a half, or two miles from Mr. Harris'; he lives in the Hampstead-road; they went up the gateway to Linton's brewery, and set the cask down at Linton's door - they then went to a workshop in the same yard, next door to Linton's, made some inquiry, and then returned up the yard; I went to them, and asked what they had left the cask there for - they appeared confused, and I said, "What business had you to leave that cask there?" they said they had found it in Smithfield, and were going to take it home- I think it was Fagan who said he picked it up, and Culley said he was assisting him to carry it; it was a small cask, and did not require two men to carry it; I took them in charge, and fetched the cask from Linton's door.
ANDREW LLOYD. I am an officer, and was with Duke- his evidence is correct; here is the cask - it has "T. Harris, Hampstead-road," on it, in large letters.
THOMAS BASSETT. I am employed by brewers to watch their property. I was with the officers when this cask was taken.
THOMAS HARRIS. I am a brewer, and live in Hampstead-road. Fagan has lived in my service, and knew my premises well. This is my cask - it is worth 8s. 6d. I have lost a considerable number.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. Fagan had been in your employ? A. Occasionally; Culley was not in my service - we loose casks in a variety of ways.
MR. ALLEY. Q. Do you ever allow a cask to be sold? A. No; that is a fixed rule in the trade.
THOMAS BISHOP. I live near Linton. The prisoners came to me on the evening in question, and asked if Linton was within - I said I believed he was; they went into the yard, returned, and said he was not; Fagan asked if I knew what public-house he used; I said I believed he was at the Fox, at the corner.
CULLEY'S Defence. This man asked me to help him.
FAGAN'S Defence. I picked up the cask.
FAGAN - GUILTY. Aged 37.
CULLEY - GUILTY. Aged 44.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280410-211]
1003. THOMAS FAGAN was again indicted for stealing, on the 28th of February, 3 casks, value 24s., the goods of Thomas Harris; and SAMUEL LINTON was indicted for feloniously receiving the same, knowing them to have been stolen.
THOMAS PERRY. I am drayman to the prisoner Linton, who carries on business at Islington-green; he sells small casks of table-beer - I have seen Fagan there several times, selling casks. One day about the 30th of November, he brought a porter cask, marked "T. Harris, Hampstead-road;" he saw Linton, and said, "I have brought you one of your casks:" they walked out together, as if they were going to have a glass; Linton used to pay 2s. for them; I saw no money pass that day. About December Fagan came and sat a cask down - Linton came and asked if I had any money - I gave him 6s.; he gave Fagan 2s. for it - it was marked with Mr. Harris' name; Fagan came to me when I was washing casks in the yard, and said, "Is it not a shame, the old man does not allow more than 2s. for the casks - he sells them for 4s. 6d."
Cross-examined by MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. How long have you lived with Linton? A. Four or five months; I left last Saturday week: before that I lived with Mr. Mitchell, of Hackney.
Q. Were you discharged for dishonesty? A. I am not compelled to read my life to you; I was discharged once: I had been about three weeks with Linton when Fagan brought the first cask - I did not inform Mr. Harris of it.
I went-to Brewer's: Hall about February, and told Bassett, for I thought it high time it was stopped. I never bought a cask - I warned Linton of it several times. I have been desired several times to fetch casks for him, but would not do it.
COURT. Q. You have been in the trade a long time? A. Yes; I believe the casks cost 8s. each, new - they are better for being new.
Mr. ALLEY declined proceeding any further in this case.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280410-212]
1003. SAMUEL LINTON was again indicted for feloniously receiving, on the 28th of February, of a certain evil disposed person, 1 barrel, value 15s., the goods of William Simpson, which had been by the said evil-disposed person stolen, he, the said Samuel Linton, well knowing it to have been stolen, against the Statute, &c.
ROBERT DUKE. On the 28th of February, I went with Bassett and Lloyd to Linton's premises, at Islingtongreen; I found about one hundred and fifty casks there, or more; I took away one barrel, one kilderkin, and two firkins - I took them to the Red Lion public-house, Islingtongreen, and saw them locked up in a coal-cellar - I desired that no person might have them but myself, or Lloyd; when we fetched them away, I found part of the head of the barrel, where the name had been, chipped off - I had made no alteration in it; one head was out when I found it at Linton's.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Was Linton present when you took them away? A. Yes; I did not ask his leave.
ANDREW LLOYD. I am an officer of Hatton-garden. I assisted in taking the casks from Linton's; when I saw them next day they were in a different state - Linton was not present then; I asked him when we found where he had got them; he gave no answer.
JOSEPH MASON PAUL. I am a cooper. On the 28th or 29th of February, the prisoner came to my shop, and told me to bring my tools; I went with him to the Red Lion, Islington-green; he went into the bar, and spoke to the landlord; the boy got the key, and went into the cellar - the casks were brought out to me in the skittle-ground; he told me to take the two little marks out - they were scratch marks; the same as we mark all our heads with - that makes the wood look clean; I never did a job for him before.
COURT. Q. Look at this cask, is it the one out of which you took two marks? A. It is; one head was out then; I asked him if it was his own; he said Yes, and told me to take them down to the shop, and cobble them.
Cross-examined. Q. What do you know the cask by? A. By the top; I know no mark on it - I do not swear positively to it; I have known Linton twelve or fourteen years, but did no business for him; he did not ask me to do this privately.
MR. ALLEY. Q. What time of day was this? A. Between six and seven o'clock in the morning of the 29th of February - he said he had bought them.
JAMES HARDING. I am servant at the Red Lion. Paul had the same casks to cut as the officers brought - they brought three; Paul left them in the skittle-ground, and the officers fetched them from the ground; when the marks were cut out, the prisoner asked me to disguise the places where they had been cut out; I was to get some dirty water and mud, and put on it; he said he would give me 6d. - I did not do it; they were fetched away in two or three hours.
Cross-examined. Q. Had you the key of the cellar? A. I got it from my master; he did not hesitate about giving it to me; I told him Linton wanted it - my master was not there when they were brought, and did not know of their being stolen.
THOMAS BASSETT. I am inspector to the brewers. I went to Linton's, on the 28th of February, with the officers; I saw this cask in the brewhouse; we took it to the public-house with others - there were about one hundred and fifty casks there with the names chipped out - there were about three hundred casks in all - this one was without a head; I went again on the 21st of March, and found the head in the brewhouse, under the same roof as the cask was - I produce it.
Cross-examined. Q. Is Mr. Simpson's name on it? A. Yes; here is "Simpson, Lambeth," on the head - it was stowed away.
WILLIAM SIMPSON. I am a brewer, and live at Lambeth. I have lost a great many casks of all descriptions - I never sold one; this head belongs to the cask produced, and is mine; another mark on the other head has been partly scraped out - it is worth about 1l.
Cross-examined. Q. How many other persons are there of your name in the trade? A. Not one.
GUILTY. Aged 68.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18280410-213]
1004. WILLIAM FARMER was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of February, 1 cask, value 6s., the goods of Job West and James West.
ROBERT DUKE. I am an officer. On the 28th of February, as I stood at the corner of Lloyd's-row, I saw the prisoner with a man, named Bateman, whom I know to be a receiver; while I was talking to Bateman the prisoner came up: Bateman called to him, "Farmer, Farmer;" I stopped him, and insisted that he should not say any thing- the prisoner went, and put down a cask at Bateman's door; I went to him, and said, "What did you leave that there for?" he said he lived with Mr. West, and that the dray was coming presently - I secured him.
JOB WEST. I am in partnership with James West; the prisoner was never in our employ - I never directed our dray to call at Bateman's - we have lost several casks.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. Was the prisoner with your drayman that day? A. No; my dray was out.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was with the man all day, and picked up this cask - I did not know it was Mr. West's; I did not know Bateman; I was going to take the cask to my lodgings, and thought I would go to the drayman next day, and give it him, as I used to go round with him.
GUILTY. Aged 44.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280410-214]
1005. WILLIAM BULL was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of February, 107 squares of glass, value 20l., and 1 box, value 6s., the goods of Thomas Kinner.
SECOND COUNT, stating it to be the goods of Henry Brooks.
MR. ALLEY conducted the prosecution.
HENRY BROOKS. I live in the Strand, and am a window glass cutter. On the 26th of January, I received an order to send these goods to Mr. Joseph Cooper, at Reading - there was some stained glass among it. I sent it on the 4th of February, by Ford, to Kinner's waggon.
GEORGE FORD. I am Mr. Brooks' servant, and took the box on the 4th of February, I delivered it to Kinner's waggon.
THOMAS KINNER. I am part owner of the Reading waggon. I received this box, and loaded it in the hind part of the waggon; which I drove, and saw it safe at the further end of Brentford. I missed it when I arrived at Hounslow; the cords had been cut to get it.
JOHN SELLS. I am a carpenter, and live at Kensington. The prisoner is a stable-keeper, and lived over Mr. Dyer's stables, at Kensington; on the morning of the 16th of February, (as near as I can judge) he came to me in the front of my house, and asked if I knew anybody in the building line, who wanted any window-glass. I said I had a house to glaze, and I would look at it - he said if I would call in about half an hour, he would shew it to me; that he had it to sell for a poor man who was in the Bench- he called in about half an hour and we went up to his room over the stable. I saw Matthews in the room, looking over the glass; I did not examine it, for I was afraid of breaking it; it looked very good. Matthews said, if he had all the money in the world, he would not give more than 6l. for it; the prisoner was in the room at that time, I think. I said nothing to him about the price - I never offered him any price; he never asked what I would give for it.
Q. Did you ever propose any price to the prisoner? A. Never; till I left the money in the publican's hands; and said if he would take 5l. or five guineas for it, I would have it.
Q. How came you to leave that message with him? A. I was obliged to go to town - I gave him four sovereigns, and asked him to lend me 25s. I got the glass out of the room on the Sunday morning afterwards; I carried it home, and had it glazed - I bought nothing but plain glass of him; the prisoner had mentioned no price in my hearing.
Cross-examined by MR. CLARKSON. Q. Did not the prisoner tell you he had it from a person at Kensington, who had it from a man in the Bench? A. No; I had known the prisoner for about a fortnight before.
JOHN MATTHEWS. I am a painter and glazier. On the 16th of February, the prisoner told me he had some glass to dispose of, for a person in trouble, and asked if I could get a customer, or buy it myself; he would let me have it reasonable - I went, and while I was measuring it, Sells came; I should call it second glass. I saw some orange coloured, and some ground glass there - there was about 120 square feet altogether; the largest square I saw was twenty-two inches by fifteen.
Cross-examined. Q. Did the prisoner keep a liverystable, in Earl-street? A. Yes; I know one Cox - I do not know where he is, or whether he has absconded; the prisoner did not tell me it came from Cox.
JAMES JONES. I am a publican. I paid the prisoner five guineas, which Sells left for him; he did not say it was for glass.
JOSEPH DYER. I am a publican; the prisoner lived in some premises of mine - I took him a pot of hot one morning, and saw a quantity of glass of different sizes; I did not notice any coloured. I asked where he got it; he said he had it to sell for one Rodman, who had bought it at a sale, at Hammersmith and left it for him to take care of, as he could not take it home; and if he got a customer he might sell it for him - the prisoner rented a stable of me, and, I believe, deals in horses.
Cross-examined. Q. Was the glass concealed? A. No; I am sure he mentioned Rodman's name.
ROBERT DUKE. I am an officer, and apprehended the prisoner; I said I had come about some glass - he said he had none. I said "No; it was some you sold to Sells;" he said he had sold some to Sells - and that he had it from a person who was now in the Bench. I asked his name, he declined answering any more questions.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280410-215]
1006. ANTHONY WEST & THOMAS SINGFIELD were indicted for stealing, on the 25th of February, 7 live tame rabbits, price 20s.; 3 live tame ducks, price 7s.; and 1 live tame drake, price 3s.; the property of AEneas Barkly.
MR. BODKIN conducted the prosecution.
JAMES AMBROSE. I am a gardner to Mr. Eneas Barkly; he lives at Highbury Grove; he has a farm-yard, where he keeps rabbits and poultry. On Sunday afternoon, on the 24th of February, at half-past four o'clock, I saw the poultry all safe, and next morning, at a quarter-past six, I missed seven rabbits, three ducks, and a drake; I have seen two of the rabbits since in Cox's custody; they were taken from a butch in a shed, which was not locked; the ducks were all white and very large, the drake was the largest ever seen.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Did your master have hand-bills printed describing them? A. Yes; I cannot read. I can swear to one rabbit by a slit in the ear; they had black spots on their faces and all over them.
MR. BODKIN. Q. How long has your master had these rabbits? A. About two years; I attended to them.
JOHN COX. I am gardener to Mr. Barkly. On the 24th of February (Sunday) these rabbits were stolen; I went to the pales about seven o'clock in the morning, and saw some footsteps; I noticed one particularly - there was one particular nail in the fore part of the heel-piece, and I traced the marks from the premises, over one field, to Hornsey-lane, about a mile towards Holloway; I do not know the prisoner - I have seen two rabbits here, which I am sure are master's.
Cross-examined. Q. Is this one of the hand-bills? A. Yes? I believe the rabbits are not correctly described, as to the colour.
MR. BODKIN. Q. Were you present when any shoes were fitted to the marks on the ground? A. Yes; they fitted exactly.
EDWARD JOHNSON. I am headborough of Islington. In consequence of information, I went to Selley's house, on the 27th of February, and received from him two live rabbits, one duck and one drake dead - I gave the rabbitsto Cox; I went on the same day to No. 12, Brand-street, Holloway, where the two prisoners live - I saw West, and asked him where Singfield was - he said he was not at home; I searched the house, and under the bed found a lot of white feathers in a flat, and then found a lot of feathers in a bag - there were about as many feathers as would cover four ducks; I left, and went there again, and saw West without his shoes; there was a pair of shoes by the side of where he sat; Price, who was with me, took them up, and I looked at them; Price said he thought they corresponded with the foot marks; we made him put them on, and took him away with them; I went next morning to Mr. Barkly's premises with the shoes, and examined the foot-marks in two or three places in the shrobbery, and fitted the shoes; they corresponded exactly with the marks. In consequence of information I went to Woburn, in Bedfordshire, and found Singfield; he saw me and ran away; I followed, and found him at Bow-brickhill, in Buckinghamshire; he was then at dinner - he said, "All you have against me is selling them; not stealing them" - and when on the coach, he said he should not have been drawn into if it had not been for Tom.
Cross-examined. Q. You have known Singfield some time? A. Yes; I never knew anything against him, but I have heard something - the ducks found were plucked. all but the heads; the drake was very large - we found some cock and hen feathers - it was on Thursday that I fitted the shoes; I think it had rained one night since the Sunday, but am not certain.
JAMES PRICE. I am an officer of Islington. On Monday morning, the 25th, I went to Mr. Barkly's and saw the footmarks; I went on Thursday and fitted the shoes; I noticed one particular nail in front of the heel, which fitted the marks exactly - West resisted being taken.
Cross-examined. Q. Was not he in bed? A. No. There had been no rain from the Sunday till the Thursday - I came up at night - there is a border round the shrobbery: the marks were very plain there.
COURT. Q. Did it appear to you that two persons had been there? A. I only saw the footmark of one person.
SAMUEL SELLEY. I bought two rabbits and two ducks of Singfield, on Monday the 25th of February, and gave the same to the officers.
JAMES PRICE. I gave a description of the rabbits as I had received it from Cox, and no other description was given for the bills.
WEST - GUILTY. Aged 28.
Transported for Seven Years.
SINGFIELD - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280410-216]
1007. ISAAC EVANS was indicted for embezzlement.
MR. ALLEY conducted the prosecution.
THOMAS STARLING. I am an engraver, and have a plan of the parish of St. Mary Islington - the prisoner was in my employ; he was to go round and shew this engraving; some of them were 5s., and some 16s., according to the size - he was to come to me every night, to state the result of his day's labour, and pay the money to me - he took a receipt-book with him and when he sold any, he was to give a receipt, and write the name on the margin of the book - he never accounted to me for monies received from Mr. Walker, Mr. Fieldhouse, and Cooper - he said they were out, and he was to call again on them.
Cross-examined by MR. CLARKSON. Q. How did you pay him? A. By a commission on every subscriber he obtained; and he was occasionally employed in writing for me; I considered him as my servant; he received the money at the time he obtained the signatures and was then to have his commission, that was, before he delivered the plans - I have no recollection of having described him as my agent; there was not a debtor and creditor account between us.
Q. Did you ever call on him to become a witness for you at any trial? A. I wished him to become a witness about a fortnight before I made this discovery; which was on the 28th of March, and not before; he was to be witness about a dozen of these plans, which he had delivered to a shopkeeper.
Q. Did he refuse to become a witness? A. The case never came on; he refused by engaging to go to Wales - I knew he was going before that - he was examined four times on this charge.
Q. Did you not, after the first examination, apply to make it up? A. No: I should have been glad to have done it, and was applied to about it by Mr. Morley; I advanced him money from time to time, as he stated that he was very poor.
JAMES WALKER. I was to have a plan from the prosecutor; I paid the prisoner 5s, for it; it was since Christmas, but I cannot say when.
CHARLES FIELDHOUSE. I ordered a plan of the prisoner, and paid him 5s. about the 11th of February.
WILLIAM COOPER. I bought this plan of the prisoner, about the first week in February, and paid him 16s.
THOMAS STARLING. These sums have never been paid to me; they are entered in the receipt-book, and he has written there that he was to call again.
Prisoner's Defence. I called on Mr. Starling on the Friday before I was taken, and said I had used about 2l. of his money, as he owed me about 6l.; I had done so repeatedly before and he had consented to it; but on that day he had an action pending - he sent for an officer, and detained me; he has been with me frequently in prison, and trifled with my feelings very much.
FOSTER EDWARD ABEL. I am articled clerk to Mr. Bean - I have received two letters from the prosecutor, in which he describes the prisoner as his agent - I have not preserved the letters.
GUILTY. Aged 23.
Transported for Seven Years.
There was another indictment against the prisoner.
[trialAccount t18280410-217]
1008. SOPHIA MATILDA CAMERON was indicted for stealing, on the 23d of February, 1 ring, value 4s., the goods of Michael McGoverin.
SARAH McGOVERIN. I am the wife of Michael Mc Goverin; we live in Union-street. The prisoner lodged with us for fourteen or fifteen weeks - I lost a ring, on the 21st of February, from a wine glass on the mantelshelf; I asked her about it several times, and desired her to give it up, but she constantly denied having it - I missed a twist gold ring about a month after; and while I went for an officer she ran away; she was afterwards taken; and the duplicate of the first ring found on her.
THOMAS PERRING. I am an officer. The prisoner wasgiven into my custody - I found three duplicates on her; one for this ring, pawned for 1s. on the 23d of February; the prosecutrix redeemed it; the pawnbroker is not here; the prisoner said she had pawned it in company with the prosecutrix's husband.
Prisoner's Defence. Her husband sent me to pawn it, as he wanted money.
MICHAEL M'GOVERIN. I never anthorized her to pawn it or anything else - I went out with her sometimes.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280410-218]
1009. JOHN HALL and THOMAS GRAY were indicted for stealing on the 22d of March, 1 silver watch, value 25s.; 1 metal key, value 2d.; and 1 metal seal, value 4d., the goods of Joseph Mayes, from his person.
JOHN MAYES. I am a watchman, of Piccadilly. On the 2d of March I was at the ruins of the Brunswick Theatre, with my wife and son, between twelve and one o'clock in the day - the crowd pressed very heavily on me, and I found my right arm confined to my side; it was done intentionally; I missed my watch from my fob as soon as my arm was at liberty - Gray was as close to my right side as he could be; I did not notice Hall till afterwards; they were both taken into custody while I was there: I am certain my watch was safe when I entered the crowd.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Was there not a moving crowd there? A. A gig came by which made them move.
GEORGE WADDINGTON. I was at the Brunswick Theatre; there was a great rush; I heard the prosecutor call out, "Don't hurt my wife;" I saw Gray had got hold of the prosecutor's coat on his right hand side, and his other hand was under the coat: Hall pushed against him close to the prosecutor, and in a moment Newton called out"He has got it" - I looked and saw the watch fall; it dropped as if from Gray, but I did not see it in any one's hand; there was a crowd at the time, in consequence of some vehicle going by.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. There was a rush backwards and forwards? A. Yes; we were all pushed against the prosecutor.
Cross-examined by MR. BODKIN. Q. Is it remarkable for a man to lay hold of another, to save himself from falling? A. He had hold of his coat, and the prosecutor called out, "Don't hurt my wife."
JAMES NEWTON. I was with Waddington, and saw Hall with his hands in a direction towards the prosecutor; he gave Gray something, which he put into his right-hand trousers pocket - Hall was in front of him, and Gray close at his right-hand side; I collared Gray directly; he put his hand into his pocket, and took something out, and the pocket with it; and in struggling I saw this watch in the kennel, between his legs; I did not see it fall - it was something small that Hall gave Gray.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Did you hear Waddington examined? A. Yes; we were all pushed together; Hall was pressed violently against the prosecutor- he could not prevent it; there were above a hundred persons there: Hall was hardly master of his hands.
Cross-examined by MR. BODKIN. Q. And yet you saw Gray put something into his pocket, and take it out again? A. Yes - there were several people pushing against us; I did not hear the watch fall; I first saw it on the ground; Gray had not been pushed about at that time- I kept him pretty steady.
WILLIAM McNAB. I went to the Theatre, and in two or three minutes there was a strong rush; Newton sang out, "George, he has got it;" I looked round, and saw the watch falling close to Gray, just by his feet; I turned round, and saw Hall on my right. I did not see the prosecutor till next day.
Cross-examined by MR. BODKIN. Q. What are you? A. A journeyman baker; I saw the watch falling. I assisted to secure Gray.
PHILIP WAINWRIGHT. I am an engraver. I was present, and saw a rush; I met Waddington, and saw Hall going through the crowd; I saw Gray putting something into his pocket, and heard Newton say, "George, he has got it;" I had seen Hall do nothing but press close to the prosecutor - his hands were towards him; I looked down, and saw the watch near the gutter. Waddington took Gray; I assisted to take Hall - I saw Newton pick the watch up.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Would it not be natural for Hall to put out his hands when there was a rush? A. Yes.
Cross-examined by MR. BODKIN. Q. You did not see Gray's hand on the prosecutor? A. No.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GRAY'S Defence. I am innocent.
GRAY - GUILTY. Aged 23.
Transported for Seven Years.
HALL - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280410-219]
1010. STEPHEN CABLE was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of March, 2 seals, value 20s.; 1 watch-key, value 6s., and 1 ring, value 4s., the goods of Samuel Tyso.
SAMUEL TYSO. I am a groom, and live with Mr. Lyons, in Holborn. On the evening of the 5th of March I was at the Green Man, public-house, in Bond-street; the prisoner, who is an acquaintance of mine, came in; we went home together to my lodging in Oxford-street - we slept together, and the next morning the prisoner got up, and went out; I missed my seals and key about half an hour after he was gone; I found him on the evening of the 7th of March, and asked him about my seals - he said he had never touched them: we went together to the Green Man, and there I gave charge of him; I saw the seals found in his watch pocket, under his own watch; they were not on his watch-chain.
Cross-examined by MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. Were you not both servants out of place? A. Yes; I got into a squabble with some girls, and went to the watch-house; the prisoner said he had advanced 2s. for me. After the seals were found he said he took them merely out of a frolic; I have known him five or six months - he has been an honest good lad.
JOHN LACY. I am a beadle. I took the prisoner, and said to him, "If you know anything of these seals, give them up;" he said he knew nothing about them; I searched him, and found them in his fob.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Confined Two Months.

1012. JOSEPH CANNON and JOHN LANE were indicted for stealing, on the 1st of April, 2 lbs. weight of cheese, value 1s. 4d., the goods of Christopher Tutin.
WILLIAM WHITTINGHAM. I am an officer. On the 1st of April I saw the two prisoners together in the Strand, they passed the window of Mr. Christopher Tutin's shop, in the Strand - he is a cheesemonger. I saw Cannon take a piece of cheese, which he gave to Lane; he passed the shop, turned short round, and took another piece, which he gave to Lane, who put them both into his apron, and wrapped it round his body - I took them both.
Prisoner LANE. He took us into the shop, and asked how many pieces of cheese they had lost, they said one. Witness. They looked afterwards, and said there were two.
CANNON - GUILTY. Aged 16.
LANE - GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280410-222]
1013. JAMES WILKINSON was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of March, certain fixtures, viz. 2 sash-frames, value 4s.; 3 sash-weights, value 1s., and 24 panes of glass, value 10s., of, and belonging to Daniel Dyball, and fixed to a certain building of his; against the Statute.
DANIEL DYBALL. I am owner of a house in Type-court, Type-street, Grub-street; I saw it on Sunday morning the 9th of March I think - it was all safe, and the sashes were all right; I had not been there before for some time - on the Monday or Tuesday night two officers came and gave me information; I went down and missed the sashes, here are some parts of them - I have fitted them and they appear to be part of the sashes; I had employed the prisoner occasionally, and he was a tenant of mine.
THOMAS BREEDON. I am a carman. On Tuesday night the 11th of March, I was in bed, and was knocked up by some one who said there were thieves in Mr. Dyball's house next door to me; I went with Mr. Bee and examined the premises - we saw a parcel of old sashes broken, which had been taken from the second floor - we went round to Dyball's; he came with us and searched the premises; on coming away, I said, I had seen the prisoner on the Sunday, taking out some at the back premises - Dyball said, "He lives up here;" we went up to his apartment - he was in bed; they went in and I staid outside - they brought out this old frame and glass which we took to the watch-house; I did not hear the prisoner say anything.
Prisoner. Q. Is there not a thoroughfare through the empty house to mine? A. It ought to be kept fastened, but I have seen it open.
JOHN BEE. I went to the prisoner's bed-room and found these parts of sash-frames; he said nothing about them; I took him to the watch-house - I have matched the pieces to the house, they all correspond - I had information, and got up, and saw a light.
DANIEL DYBALL. I swear these are my property; they were in the fore part of the house.
Prisoner. Q. Was there a fire in my room? A. Very little, if any.
Prisoner's Defence. There is no yard to my house; I went to bed that night very soon after nine - my wife staid up; she had to go to the cellar, and seeing these pieces thrown down, she brought them up in her apron, and put some on the fire.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280410-223]
1014. WILLIAM SAMUELS was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of February, 75lbs. of lead, value 5s., the goods of Isaac Dark, and fixed to a building of his.
ISAAC DARK. I have a house at Hammersmith, in the parish of Fulham. On the 20th of February, between three and four o'clock in the morning, a person gave me information: I went to the house and missed this lead: I found one piece of lead about eighteen yards from the premises, and the rest was turned up.
RICHARD HEDGES. On the morning of the 20th of February, I was at Hammersmith and saw the prisoner and another man taking the lead off the shop front of Dark's house, I went up and spoke to the prisoner, who I knew, and they both ran away - I called, Dark, who got up and went to the premises: he got over, and went and took the prisoner up - some of the lead was torn up and chopped; I prevented them taking it away - it was about five o'clock.
WILLIAM HALL. I found this piece of lead in the road to the creek, about seven o'clock in the morning.
EDWARD EDGESON. I apprehended the prisoner, and matched part of lead to the premises - it fitted exactly.
Prisoner's Defence. I can prove I was in bed at the time.
- PRICE. I know the prisoner. On the night before the robbery he was about with me, and went to bed at half-past nine o'clock - we slept in the same bed at Mrs. Salmon's, near the water side; I left him in bed between six and seven in the morning, when I got up Mrs. Salmon saw us there - she is not able to come here; her daughter also saw us - it was on Tuesday night the, 20th of February.
RICHARD HEDGES re-examined. I swear I saw him at the house. I was close to him, and spoke to him; he goes by the name of Doggey: I said "Master Doggey, I have caught you at last" and he ran away - I told Mr. Dark who it was - there is a gas-light opposite.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280410-224]
1018. THOMAS RANDALL was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of March, 1 time-piece, value 10s., the goods of Charles Fisher.
SUSAN FISHER. I am the wife of Charles Fisher, a gunsmith, who lives in St. Ann-street, Westminster. I lost a time-piece from my mantel-shelf, on the 14th of March; I was in my shop, and my daughter was serving a man some with Spanish juice - my daughter ran out, and the man said "She has run up Pye-street." I put my head out, and saw a piece of work; I went up the street, and met my daughter at the corner of Duck-yard; she said,"I have lost sight of him;" we came back, and I saw Timbrell, and spoke to him; he went away, and before he came back, some people came and said, "Your timepiece is on the dung-hill, in Duck-yard;" it was soon afterwards brought to me. I had seen it safe a short time before.
SUSAN FISHER. I am the prosecutor's daughter. On the 14th of March, I was in the shop, serving, and I saw the prisoner in the parlour; I went to ask him what he wanted, and I saw he had the time-piece in his hand - he ran out, and I pursued him; he gave the time-piece to another boy in Pye-street - they went up the street together, and I lost sight of them - I saw the prisoner in custody on the Friday night.
THOMAS HORTON. I was working in Duck-yard, and picked up this time-piece, which I gave to the prosecutrix.
BENJAMIN TIMBRELL. I am an officer. I took the prisoner in Strutton-ground.
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Transported for Seven Years.

1016. JAMES NEWMAN, ROBERT NEWMAN, and JAMES BOLDING were indicted for stealing, on the 9th of March, 1 beam of wood, value 3s.; 1 deal spar, value 9d., and a piece of oak, value 1s., the goods of Daniel Dyball.
DANIEL DYBALL. I had some timber lying about my premises; there was a beam twelve feet long, lying in Type-court - I saw it safe on the 9th of March, and missed it next day. I went about ten o'clock to Newman's lodging, which is at the back of these premises, and found this wood in the cupboard - James Newman and his mother were not in the room; Robert (the father) was not there - James and his mother refused to let us search; we secured them - the beam had then been cut.
THOMAS BREEDEN. When Wilkinson was taken, I happened to say, "I saw Bolding and John Newman lifting up some wood into the window of Newman's place;" it had a piece of iron to it.
GEORGE WILLIAM BENNETT. I am a constable. On Wednesday morning, the 12th of March, I went to Newman's room, and asked James where Dyball's property was; they said they did not know what I meant; I found about half a cart-load of wood in the cupboard, and some wood was on the fire - Mr. Dyball selected a beam, a spar, and a piece of oak.
JOHN BEE. I was also at the house, and found the wood.
DANIEL DYBALL. I knew the wood, because it matched with the rest, and having seen it a long time; I have put a fence eight feet high round the premises - nobody can get there, except through my house.
ROBERT NEWMAN'S Defence. The wood came from two houses which had fallen down; the houses are in Chancery, and every body takes refuge there.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280410-226]
1017. JOSEPH LEONARD was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of March, 6 yard of carpet, value 20s., the goods of Richard Hewitt.
JAMES BOYLEN. I am servant to Mr. Richard Hewitt, cabinet-maker of Hammersmith. On the 21st of March, an alarm was given, and a piece of carpet was missed from the front of the shop; I ran out and took the prisoner, between three and four o'clock, at Kensington, with it - I did not see him till I got there; he had it under his left arm- I went up to him, and said I had every reason to believe it was masters; I secured him. I had seen it safe that morning; I left it at the watch-house with Mrs. Bennett.
RICHARD HEWITT. This is my carpet. I went out about one o'clock, and left it safe then - I have a piece here, which matches it.
WILLIAM BENNETT. I am watch-house-keeper of Kensington. I came home about half an hour after the prisoner was taken my wife gave me this carpet.
Prisoner's Defence. There are a hundred carpets alike- I do not see how he can swear to it.
GUILTY. Aged 27.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280410-227]
1018. WILLIAM JOHNSON was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of March, 4 combs, value 7s.; 6 pencil-cases, value 8s., and 1 cigar tube, value 1s., the goods of John Conden.
MARY CONDEN. I am the wife of John Conden, a perfumer. On the 20th of March, I saw the prisoner in the shop; he was a stranger - I went and asked what he wanted; he said he came for relief - I saw the glass-case in confusion, and detained him; he then took three combs from his pocket - the officer came and took him.
THOMAS SMITH. I saw the prisoner in the shop; he gave Mrs. Conden three combs from his pocket; she left him in my care, while she fetched an officer - I asked if he had anything more; he gave me these pencil-cases and a cigar tube from his jacket pocket.
SAMUEL TAYLOR. I am an officer, and took him in charge.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 15.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280410-228]
1019. JAMES GIBSON was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of April, 1 fowl, price 2s. 6d., the property of William Beetles.
WILLIAM BEETLES. I am a bricklayer, and live in Powell-street, East. On the 6th of April, I saw all my fowls safe, and missed one in the evening; they were in an enclosed yard, and had the run of the stables; the prisoner is a carman, and attends a horse on my premises.
JOHN WILLIAMSON. On the 6th of April, I was in Pewter-platter-yard. I saw the prisoner take up a duck, and put it into his pocket; I fetched the owner of it - he gave it up, and was set at liberty; I then saw the legs of this fowl sticking out of his pocket - I told an officer, who took him, he said he bought it between Old-street, and Brick-lane.
JOHN HAYFORD. I am an officer, and took him in charge; he had a handkerchief full of salt fish and potatoes, and 1s. 8d. in his pocket - he was tipsy, and would not tell me where he got the fowl; he afterwards said he had bought it, but would not say where - Beetle's claimed it.
Prisoner's Defence. I gave 1s. 9d. for it.
GUILTY. Aged 31.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280410-229]
1020. SARAH COPELAND was indicted for stealing,on the 7th of March, 1 bed, value 15s.; 1 bolster, value 1s.; 2 sheets, value 2s.; 1 blanket, value 2s.; 1 quilt, value 6d.; 2 saucepans, value 1s. 3d.; 1 stove, value 4s.; 3 chairs, value 2s.; 1 looking-glass, value 3d.; 1 candlestick, value 3d.; 1 pair of tongs, value 3d.; 2 flat-irons, value 6d.; 1 pail, value 3d.; 1 frying-pan, value 3d., and 1 kettle, value 3d., the goods of John Godson.
LYDIA GODSON. I am the wife of John Godson, shoemaker, of Kingsland-road; the prisoner has lodged with us for three years, with her husband. On the 7th of March I met her in Thomas-street, Hackney-road, and said, "That is my chair you have got;" she said, "No, it is a friend's - I am going to take it home;" I then thought it was not mine, and went home; she did not come home all night, and next morning, we found their door open, and missed all these articles - they had never slept out before; nothing but two chairs were left; we have found nothing but two chairs; she told us where she had sold the other things, but the people had sold them again - she never returned; I went next morning, and at the corner of Kingsland-road saw her and her husband; I laid hold of him, and she ran away; I called Stop thief! and she was taken by my husband: she said she had taken the things, and pawned and sold them.
PHILLIS BOWEN. I saw the prisoner take two chairs by my window on two different evenings - I lodge in Godson's house.
THEOPHILUS WHITING. I am a constable. and took her in charge; she took me to where she had sold some things, but they were sold again.
The prisoner pleaded distress.
GUILTY. Aged 43.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18280410-230]
1021. LEONARD BROWN was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of April, 1 wooden bowl, value 2d.; 1 shilling, and 3 sixpences, the property of Richard Wall.
SUSAN PROUTING. I live with Richard Wall, a tripeman, of Market-street. On the 7th of April, I saw the prisoner in the shop, with the till open in his hand; I went into the shop, and he got under the counter - I saw the bowl by his side on the floor, with the silver in it; I took him to the watch-house.
GEORGE GILLESPIE. I received him in charge; he said he did it from distress, as he had no home.
GUILTY. Aged 15.
Whipped and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18280410-231]
1022. CHARLES ROBINSON was indicted for stealing, on the 15th of March, 4 lbs. of cheese, value 2s., the goods of Thomas Harland.
WILLIAM NEWMAN. I am servant to Thomas Harland, a cheesemonger, of St. John-street, Clerkenwell. On the 15th of March, about four o'clock in the afternoon, my boy was going out to take some cheese, and gave me information; I went to the door, and saw the prisoner with this cheese, going from the shop; the boy took hold of him, and brought him back; we took it from him - he said he would never do the like again. Mr. Harland let him go, and in about half an hour he returned, and took another piece out of the window; Hocker brought him back, and I took it from under his coat - he was a little in liquor.
SAMUEL HOCKER. I am a butcher, and live opposite the prosecutor. On Saturday afternoon I was at my door, and saw the prisoner brought back with the cheese; they pardoned him, and he left; but in about twenty minutes I saw him return, and go staggering up to the shop; he took this piece of cheese with a ticket on it - he deliberately took the ticket off, and put the cheese under his arm; I crossed over, collared him, and took him back.
JOHN ROBINSON. I am an officer, and took him in charge; I found a picture and an Italian iron on him.
The prisoner put in a written defence, pleading intoxication.
GUILTY. Aged 26.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280410-232]
1023. THOMAS NICHOLSON and SARAH NICHOLSON were indicted for stealing, on the 3d of April, 1 sheet, value 2s.; 1 bolster, value 5s.; 1 quilt, value 2s., and 1 set of bed-furniture, value 10s., the goods of Peter Ring.
PETER RING. The prisoners (who are married) lodged with me for a fortnight, till the 3d of April, when they were taken up on another charge; I then searched the room, and missed all these articles.
GEORGE UNDERWOOD. I am a pawnbroker; I produce a blanket and sheet, pawned on the 17th and 18th of March by the female prisoner.
WILLIAM CREE. I am a pawnbroker. I have a bolster, pawned by the male prisoner.
JOSEPH GODDARD. I am a pawnbroker. I have a set of bed-furniture pawned by the man, and a quilt by the woman.
WILLIAM KIRBY. I am an officer. I took the prisoners, and found the duplicates of the property on the man.(Property produced and sworn to.)
THOMAS NICHOLSON'S Defence. I have been in business, and was very unfortunate; I kept a banker, but was driven to the greatest distress.
THOMAS NICHOLSON - GUILTY. Aged 36.
Confined One Month.
SARAH NICHOLSON - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280410-233]
1024. WILLIAM HALE was indicted for stealing, on the 3d of April, 38lbs. of beef, value 19s., the goods of William Mathews.
WILLIAM MATHEWS. I live in St. Giles'. On the 3d of April, I saw the prisoner coming from my slaughter-house, with this beef under his apron - he was not in my employ; I pursued, and said, "What have you there?" he began to run; and threw it down the flap of an area, which was open; I caught him about two hundred yards off - two men brought me the beef - it was cut off a bullock in my slaughter-house.
EDWARD ETTERIDGE. I am in Mathew's employ; I drove the cart into the yard, and saw the prisoner come out of the slaughter-house with the beef; master pursued, and took him - I am certain he is the man.
HENRY BATES. I saw the prisoner throw the beef down an area in St. Andrew-street, and I took it up.
Prisoner's Defence. I met a man, who asked me to lend him my apron - he went into the slaughter-house, and brought out the beef to me, to take to Monmouth-street.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 39.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18280410-234]
1025. JOHN FLEMING was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of March, 3 shillings, the monies of Charles Fenn.
CHARLES FENN. I am a cheesemonger, and live in the City-road. The prisoner was in my employ; on the 13th of March, in consequence of information, I marked 26 shillings, and put them into my till, about half-past twelve in the day - I remained in the shop till half-past one, and then went to dinner - on my return, I found one shilling gone - I sent him to his dinner, and then marked two shillings more; and about three o'clock I went out, calling him to mind the shop; I returned in a quarter of an hour, and in that time I found he had taken about 8s. 6d., but on examining the till, there were only twenty-five marked shillings, & seven shillings not marked- I stated to him that I had missed money; he said he knew nothing about it - I said I must send for an officer - he then pointed to where he had concealed some money.
Cross-examined. by MR. BARRY. Q. Did you not tell him, it would be better to confess it? A. No; I said I did not wish him to confess it.
COURT. Q. Where did be point? A. To some straw under the counter - I saw Angel examine the straw, and take a shilling out - two officers came, one of whom went up with me to examine the prisoner's box, and on the top of it he found two shillings and some copper; and at the bottom of it two marked shillings, and other monies; here is one of the shillings.
WILLIAM ATTFIELD. I am an officer. Angel gave me a marked shilling - I found these other two up stairs.
JOSEPH ANGEL. I lodge at Mr. Fenn's. The prisoner pointed to the straw, and said he had put three shillings there - I could find but one.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you not frighten him? A. No.
THOMAS VANN. I took the prisoner into custody - he seemed much distressed, and wanted to know what would become of him; he said he never robbed his master before, and that two of the shillings were in his box, of which he gave me the key.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Strongly recommended to mercy - Confined 3 Months.
[trialAccount t18280410-235]
1026. MARY GRIFFITHS was indicted for stealing, on the 22d of March, 1 leg of pork, value 2s., and 1 hand of pork, value 1s. 6d., the goods of William Stoker.
WILLIAM STOKER. I kept a pork-shop in Bethnal Green. On the 22d of March, I was crossing to my own house, and saw the prisoner come out of my shop; she looked in at the window, then turned the corner, into another street - I went in and asked my wife if she had sold anything, she said, No; I went out and saw the prisoner with another woman - they separated on seeing me, and went up to the prisoner who had a basket; I asked what she had there; she said, "That woman gave it to me; she opened the basket, and there was a hand of pork in it - I said, "Have you not got a leg?" she said, she knew nothing of that - I took her back to the shop; I went for an officer, and when I returned, she had dropped a leg of pork from under her clothes; I am sure she is the woman who came out of the shop.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Did you see her within the shop? A. I saw her just on the top step of the door; I did not say she was holding up her gown with her other hand - she had a great cloak on.
WILLIAM HAWKINS. I am a butcher. On the 22d of March, I was standing two doors from the prosecutor's shop and saw a crowd there; I went up and saw the leg of pork drop from under the prisoner's cloak - I took it up.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Had she been searched before? A. No; she was near the counter - I am certain it did not fall from the counter; the basket was on the counter.
HENRY RICE. I am a constable. I saw a mob and went into this house, and took the prisoner.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Fourteen Days.
[trialAccount t18280410-236]
1029. EDWARD HEARNE was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of April, 5lbs. weight of rope, value 2s., of the goods and merchandize of George Reeves, and being in a certain barge on a certain navigable canal, called the Regent's Canal; against the Statute.
WILLIAM TURNER. I am a lighterman; this rope belongs to George Reeves and Co.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280410-237]
1027. THOMAS WILSON was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of March, 1 rug, value 5s., the goods of the Right Honourable Lord Stewart De Rothesay.
JAMES SHERBORNE. I am in the service of Lord Stewart De Rothesay. On the 27th of March, I was standing at the corner of Brook-street, on the carriage-box, and saw the prisoner come and take the rug from the carriage; I called, Stop thief! and pursued him - he threw it down; I am sure he is the man.
CHARLES MARTIN. I am a watchman; I was on duty in Hanover-square - I heard a cry of Stop thief! I saw the prisoner running in Brook-street - he was stopped in Maddox-street, and I took him,(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 28.
