[trialAccount t18280410-238]
1029. GEORGE LEITH was indicted for stealing, on the 22d of February, 1 hat, value, 8s. the goods of Richard Bowden.
RICHARD BOWDEN. I am a hatter. On the 22d of February the prisoner was brought into my shop with this hat, which he had taken from my door - he said he was in distress, and did not care what became of him; I gave him a shilling and let him go; he was afterwards taken by the street-keeper.
ANDREW WRIGHT. I saw the prisoner take the hat from Mr. Bowden's window, in High-street, St. Giles's; Mr. Bowden gave him 1s.; he said he would not go away, but collected a mob, and the beadle took him.
GEORGE POND. I am an officer. I saw the prisoner, with a mob round him - he was told to go on, but he said he would rather be taken.
Prisoner. I wished to he tried, as I did not know what I should do after I had spent my shilling.
GUILTY. Aged 23.
Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18280410-239]
1030. HENRY PIERSEY GARNSEY was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of March, 1 coat, value 30s., the goods of William Shearman.
WILLIAM SHEARMAN. On the 23d of March, in the evening, I left a coat at the bar of the Nelson's Head public-house, Hackney-road, while I took a pint of beer; I did not see it taken.
JOSEPH THIMBLEBY. I am a pawnbroker. This coat was pawned with me on the 24th of March, by the prisoner.
ELIZABETH HANNAH WRIGHT. I live at the Nelson's Head. On the 23d of March, about half-past ten o'clock at night, I was in the parlour, and saw the prisoner coming out of the bar - he turned by the counter; I went and looked at the till - that was all right; I did not think of the coat till the prosecutor applied for it.
GEORGE SMITH. I took the prisoner, and found the duplicate on him.
Prisoner's Defence. I never took the coat; I met a man who asked me to pawn it for him, as he did not like to go into a pawnbroker's; and he gave me the ticket.
GUILTY. Aged 68.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280410-240]
1031. MARY DOBIE was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of March, 1 lace cap, value 13s.; and 1 drinking glass, value 8d.; the goods of Joseph Warf.
MARIA WARF. I am wife of Joseph Warf - we live at Wapping. On the 20th of March I met the prisoner - she said she was very much distressed; and I employed her to wash for me - on the 24th I missed these things, and charged her with it; she said, "You don't know what you have lost - if you won't give me in charge I will make it all right."
JOHN HENRY HARVEY. I am an officer. I took the prisoner into custody; but the property has not been found.
Prisoner. I never saw them.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280410-241]
FIFTH DAY. TUESDAY, APRIL 15.
Third Middlesex Jury - before Mr. Common Serjeant.
1032. AMELIA LEVY was indicted for stealing, 4 pieces of ribbon, value 4s. the goods of William Petherbridge.
The prisoner pleaded GUILTY.
Fined One Shilling and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18280410-242]
1033. JAMES ELLIS, WILLIAM BOYDEN, and THOMAS WILLIAMS, was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of February, 1 truck, value 4l., the goods of James Sholbred and Gregory Cook.
JAMES SHOLBRED. I am in partnership with Gregory Cook, of Tottenham-court-road. We lent this truck to Maynard; I have since seen the front part of it, with our name on it.
JAMES MAYNARD. I borrowed the truck on the 28th of February, and at half past nine o'clock at night put it into the mews - I missed it in the morning.
FRANCIS KEYS. I am an officer. On the 29th of February, I found the front of this truck in the lost of a stable, in Great Garden-street, Whitechapel; Ellis stood at the top of the mews, the other two were in the stable - the front of the truck was smeared over with dirt; I cleaned it, and found the prosecutor's name on it.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280410-243]
1034. JAMES ELLIS, THOMAS WILLIAMS, and WILLIAM BOYDEN were again indicted for stealing, on the 29th of February, 1 cart, value 14l., the goods of James Prichard.
JAMES PRICHARD. On the 28th of February, about eleven o'clock in the evening, I put my cart into my shed in Paxton-place, Tottenham-court-road; I found it at Lambeth-street Office a good deal broken - I can identify it as mine; my name was nearly taken off the shaft, and the copse was gone.
THOMAS LLOYD. On the 29th of February, about a quarter past seven o'clock in the morning, I saw Ellis, in Gun-square, Houndsditch, sawing the copse board of a cart - Boyden was unscrewing one side of the cart; I did not stay there above five minutes.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. This is a public place? A. Yes - I saw the name of "James Prichard, Fitzroy-market," on the cart.
COURT. Q. Was that part sawed off? A. They were sawing it off at the time.
GEORGE EWING. One day in February I was at my own door in Gun-square, and heard a cart coming up - I looked out, and saw Ellis; he asked if I knew anybody who wanted to buy a cart - I said, No; he said, "You don't want to buy it?" - I said No, and asked if he had had it long - he said No, that he took it of his brother for a debt.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you see his brother? A. I saw a man looking down the street, who it was I do not know; I returned about eleven o'clock, and saw Ellis and another man at the King's Arms public-house door, talking about a debt - they asked me to drink with them, and I did.
FRANCIS KEYS. I was on duty in Whitechapel on the 29th of February, between eleven and twelve o'clock in the morning, and saw Boyden and Phillips go out of Ellis's stable with a horse and an old cart, with the name of James Ellis on it; I followed them, and when they got to Aldgate church I lost them - I waited about Whitechapel church for nearly an hour, and saw Ellis driving down the cart in question - the other two were in the body of the cart; they drove down to the mews in Great Garden-street, and just before they got to the stable I saw Boyden get out; I got my brother officer - we went and found Ellis at the top of the mews; we took him to the stable - the other two were lying there - we handcuffed them together; I asked whose cart that was - neither of them would own it; I said to Ellis, "I suppose it is yours" - he said it was, and that the horse was his; I asked where he got the cart - he said he had it from his brother for a debt; they had gone out in an old cart, but came home in this one; which the prosecutor claims; the sideboard and spring were taken off, and the name on the shaft partly obliterated and covered with grease - I could just trace the name of James on it - the front board was off.
JAMES FOWLER. I saw the prisoners driving up Whitechapel in the cart which Mr. Prichard claimed; part of the copse had been sawed off, the boards on each side taken off, the springs taken off, and a pair of wooden ones put on.
Cross-examined. Q. That was in a public place. A. Yes.
JOSEPH HAMMOND. I was with the other officers - their evidence is correct.
ELLIS'S Defence. I sold my brother a horse the week before, for 3l. 10s., and on this day I met him in Fenchurch-street, and asked him for 30s. which he owed me - he said he would pay me in the morning, for he had bought a cart cheap at a sale, and was going to Smithfield to sell it; I said I wanted one, but his would come to too much - I went and offered it to Ewing, he would not buy it; my brother then said he would take off the springs, and make it come cheaper, and agreed to let me have it for one sovereign, besides what he owed me; we went to the King's Head, and had a drop of beer; I took the name off, and put my own on.
BOYDEN'S Defence. I took the rails off the cart by Ellis's direction.
GEORGE EWING re-examined. He did bring the cart to me - he had worked for me nearly twelve months, and was very honest.
FRANCIS KEYS. I got the springs of the cart three days afterwards from one Brown; there was the name of Ellis on a tin plate in the cart.
BOYDEN - GUILTY. Aged 19.
ELLIS - GUILTY. Aged 24.
Transported for Seven Years.
WILLIAMS - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280410-244]
1035. PETER DRAPER was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of March, 1 box, value 5s.; 1 cap, value 1l.; 5 towels, value 5s., and 7 lbs. weight of butter, value 5s., the goods of Henry Johnston.
FRANCIS KEYS. I am an officer. I was on duty in Whitechapel on the 13th of March, and saw the prisoner take this box out of a truck, and run away with it; I pursued, and took him. The box was tied, and locked; I opened it, and found a cap, five lumps of butter, and five towels in it. I have seen the prosecutor, who claimed the property, but he said he was going to sea, and could not come here.
JOHN DOWNEY. I am a porter. I had an order from Mr. Ogilby, a ship agent, to go and get this box from Horsleydown, for Mr. Henry Johnston; I was going along Whitechapel with it in a truck - I missed the box, and called out to the people to know if they had seen it; the officer came up, and told me to go to the watch-house, where I saw it.
JAMES STACE. I was with Keys, and saw the prisoner walk up to the truck, and take the box out; it was directed to Mr. Henry Johnston.
Prisoner's Defence. I was very much intoxicated, and did not know where I was till the next morning.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280410-245]
1036. THOMAS HILL was indicted for stealing, on the 3d of April, 3 sovereigns, the monies of Charles Flackney, from his person.
CHARLES FLACKNEY. On the 3d of April, about eight o'clock at night, I was within eighty or ninety yards of my own door, in Bethnal-green-road, when I lost three sovereigns from my right-hand breeches pocket - I saw the prisoner take them; I had been to Oxford-market, to draw one quarter's pension - I received three sovereigns, a crownpiece, and some copper; I had some refreshment there, and returned towards home.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Were you sober? A. I was - I had been to four public-houses, and drank at all of them, but very little; I went to two public-houses in Holborn, and then to the Black Dog, public-house, Bethnal-green: I went to two houses before I met him; my apprentice fetched me from the Black Dog about eight o'clock; the prisoner followed me out, saying he would go home with me; I said I would go alone, but he said he would go, as he had come so far. I was certainly the worse for liquor, but not drunk.
THOMAS SHAW. I am a weaver. Between eight and nine o'clock on the 3d of April I heard a cry of Stop thief! and saw the prisoner running - he stooped down at Short's window, and I heard something chink on the iron bars; I went home, and told my father, who sent me there next morning, and I saw three sovereigns down the area - I saw Capon, and told him of it.
Cross-examined. Q. Was it not rather dark? A. Yes- several people were running, but the prisoner was first.
WILLIAM CAPON. I am the prosecutor's apprentice; my mistress sent me to look for my master; I saw him and the prisoner sitting at the Black Dog; I went home, and told mistress master would not come - she sent me there again; I found the prisoner still with him - the prisoner came out just after us, and kept behind us till we got near my master's; my master was talking; I saw the prisoner spring from my master's left side to his right, but I did not see him take anything; my master turned round, and called Stop thief! the prisoner ran by Mr. Short's, and was taken two doors beyond there.
Cross-examined. Q. Were there people before the prisoner? A. Yes, but not running; he was not tipsy, but a little fresh; for he could run after the prisoner.
LEWIS SHORT. I am a shoemaker, and live in Bethnalgreen-road. On Good Friday morning the prosecutor came to my door early, and I found three sovereigns in my area, which I gave him; there are iron-railings over my area.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 55.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18280410-246]
1037. JOHN SHIELDS was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of March, 1 handkerchief, value 3s., the goods of Samuel William Thomas, from his person.
SAMUEL WILLIAM THOMAS. I was by the Horse Guards on the 16th of March - Boston came and said I had lost my handkerchief - I felt, and missed it; the prisoner was taken into custody.
JOHN BOSTON. I was in St. James' Park, and saw the prisoner, with Butcher and Clifford; they went up to the prosecutor, and I saw them turn from him; I and my brother officer seized them, and I saw this handkerchief fall down between them.
THOMAS ROBERTS. I was there that morning at the time of relieving guard; I saw the prisoner take the handkerchief out of the prosecutor's pocket - the others were close to him; they were all seized, but the bill was thrown out against the others.
WILLIAM WHITTINGHAM. I was in Parliament-street, and saw the prisoner with the others, but I did not see them taken.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I never picked the gentleman's pocket; Boston and Roberts cannot be believed; one of them was here for perjuring himself, and the other for lead stealing.
JOHN BOSTON. I was tried here about five years ago for a lamp which I pawned while I was in service; I was in prison three months, and since then I have got a little place as porter at an upholsterer's.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280410-247]
Before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
1038. ANN COPELAND was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of April, 1 watch, value 30s.; 2 seals, value 10s.; 1 ring, value 2s.; 1 watch-key, value 4s.; 1 cross, value 4s., and 1 watch-ribbon, value 1d., the goods of John Miller, from his person.
JOHN MILLER. I am a seaman. I met the prisoner on the 8th of April, a little after two o'clock in the morning, in Ratcliff-highway. I had been out with a friend, but was quite sober; the prisoner was sitting down in the street; she accosted me, and asked me to go with her- we went to No. 4, Angel-gardens; we went into a room; I asked her if she had any key, that I might lock the door - she produced one which would lock it outside, but not inside; we went to bed with the door latched; I was in bed about three hours - when I awoke she was gone: I went to my clothes, and missed my watch and 10s. in silver; my watch had been in my fob, and the silver in the left pocket of my trousers, which were in the chair at the foot of the bed - I am sure they were safe when I went to bed; I went down to the door, and saw a watchman - I asked him what place it was, and told him what I had lost. I saw the prisoner in custody in the afternoon; my ribbon and ring were found on her person.
HENRY WADE. I am a pawnbroker. This watch was brought into our shop by the prisoner and another woman, to be pawned, and I stopped it; the prisoner said the person it belonged to was in a public-house; she went for him, and was taken.
BENJAMIN BLABY. I am an officer, and took the prisoner on the 8th of April; I found this ribbon and ring on her.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. He told me he had no money but some copper, and I saw none.
GUILTY. Aged 31.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18280410-248]
1039. MARY BROWN was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of April, 2 steel plates, value 13s., the goods of John Hurdlestone, from his person.
JOHN HURDLESTONE. I am a manufacturer of gold and silver lace. On the 8th of April I had been to take some lace home, and was returning to my house about half-past ten o'clock at night; I was in Whitechapel, the prisoner came up to me, and asked me to go with her; before I could speak to her, I found her hand in my right hand breeches pocket; I pulled it out - she ran away, and I immediately missed two steel plates from the pocket which her hand had been into - I pursued her, she ran into a young man's arms; I went to take her and the man knocked me down. I got up, and still pursued, but could hardly keep sight of her - I ran nearly a mile, till I could run no further; and then I called to a watchman to stop her, which he did. I had not lost sight of her; she went behind a man and woman, and was taken - one of the plates was found in the road, along which she had run.
HENRY PHILLIPS. I am a watchman. I heard the prosecutor cry Stop thief! and saw the prisoner running in Old Castle-street, towards Wentworth-street; I pursued, and stopped her - the prosecutor came up, and said she was the girl.
MOSELY GLOVER. I am a watchman. I picked up this steel plate, at the top of Old Castle-street; the other has not been found.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was going down Castle-alley, and this man was standing there; he took hold of me, and said, "Now, I have got you;" I said, "I will give myself up to the watchman;" he came up, and said "What is the matter?" I said, "This man says I have robbed him, and I will give myself up to you;" the prosecutor said, "You were with another girl, who took my plates out of my pocket."
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18280410-249]
1040. WILLIAM JONES was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of April, 2 files, value 9d., the goods of Joseph Elliott; and 32 squares of glass, value 2l. 8s., the goods of John Elliott; and fixed to a certain building of his; against the Statute.
CHARLES GOODMAN. I am a watchman, of Islington. I was on duty, on the 7th of April, between three and four o'clock in the morning; I heard the spring of a rattle, and the cry of Stop thief! I saw the prisoner running in Cloudesley-square, and took him; I asked him what was amiss - he said, "There is nothing amiss; let me go." I took him to the watch-house; and these two files were found on him.
CHARLES LEWIS. I am a watchman. I was on duty on the night in question, in Chapman-street, and saw the prisoner in the road, with this glass in a handkerchief, under his arm; I asked him where he was going - he said he came from Highgate. I told him he must come with me to the watch-house; he threw down the glass, and broke every pane of it - he then bolted. I pursued him, and sprung my rattle.
JAMES THOMPSON. I am a watchman. I was near Lewis, and saw him stop the prisoner - I was going to him, and saw him throw down the glass, which I took up; this is it - I saw the prisoner searched, and these files found.
JOSEPH ELLIOTT. My brother's name, is John Elliott; he is building a house for a gentleman at Islington - and I was at work there; I left these two files in the house, on the Saturday evening - I particularly fastened the house up; the house had been entered, by forcing in the sashes, which were only tacked in - I suppose this glass was in the frames; but it is all broken now - the glass was gone from the frames.
Prisoner's Defence. I picked up the glass and files by the side of a wall.
GUILTY. Aged 28.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280410-250]
1041. JAMES BRIAN was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of April, 2 seals, value 3l.; 2 rings, value 1l.; part of a watch-chain, value 10s, and 1 watch-key, value 10s., the goods of Henry Westley, from his person.
HENRY WESTLEY, Esq. I am a gentleman. I was at Covent-Garden Theatre on Friday evening the 11th of April; I was handing some ladies to a carriage when the play was over; I had not shut the carriage door, intending to get in myself, but a man came up and snatched at my watch-chain; he got the seals, the rings, and a portion of the chain; he snatched with considerable force; I made an effort to take him, but he escaped under some coaches; and the prisoner knocked me down on one knee, which prevented my seizing the thief - I have no doubt whatever that it was done intentionally - I took the prisoner; he struggled very much, but I gave him in charge.
Cross-examined by MR. PRENDERGAST. Q. You did not see the prisoner near you at the time your property taken? A. Yes, they were both together, and the other took the seals; it did not occupy a minute; I did not pursue the person who took them; the prisoner did not say it was accidental; he denied that he pushed me at all; he did not say his eyes were bad, and he could not see.
EDMUND DONOVAN. I am a watchman. I took the prisoner; nothing was found on him.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18280410-251]
1042. WILLIAM JONES and GEORGE WARWICK was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of April, 6 brushes, value 9s. the goods of John Sears.
GEORGE ANSON. I live with my parents in Weston-street, Somers-town. On the 12th of April, at twelve o'clock in the day, I was playing at shuttlecock; I saw the two prisoners go up to Mr. Sears's house; Jones took these brushes off the door-post; Warwick was close by him, and had a basket in his hand; Jones put the brushes into the basket, and they went away - I told Mrs. Sears; she and I went in pursuit; we took them about 100 yards off; the brushes were found in the basket.
ANN SEARS. I am the wife of John Sears; he is a tinman, and lives in Weston-street. Anson gave me information; we pursued the prisoners; I found these six brushes of my husband's in the basket which Warwick was carrying.
ROBERT TEASDALE. I am an officer, and took the prisoners.
Warwick's Defence. I heard a cry of run; I ran, and was taken.
JONES - GUILTY. Aged 14.
WARWICK - GUILTY. Aged 14.
Confined Three Months, and Whipped.
[trialAccount t18280410-252]
1043. THOMAS JOHNSON, EVAN JONES, and JOHN SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of February, 1 cap, value 1s., the goods of James Tyer.
JAMES FOGG. I am an officer. I was with my brother in Rosemary lane on the 27th of February; I saw Johnson and Smith together; and Evans soon after joined them; they attempted several shops in Rosemary-lane; they went to a coppersmith, and two of them tried to lift up a copper from there, while the other looked in at the window; they were there about half an hour, when they left and went down the Commercial-road, and Johnson and Smith went into a shoemaker's shop, and looked at some shoes, but there were several persons there, and they came out again; they then went to Mr. Tyers, - Johnson and Smith went in; Jones remained outside, as he had done before; they staid there a little while, came out, and crossed the road; I and my brother took them, and found this cap in Johnson's hat; he said his mistress, who kept a shop in Whitechapel, had sent him to Limehouse for it.
JOHN FOGG. I was with my brother, and confirm his statement - the prisoners were all in company together.
CHARLOTTE TYER. I am wife of James Tyer. On the 27th of February I came into the shop; my sister said, some suspicious characters had been there; I then looked and missed this cap, which the officers brought in with the prisoner.
JOHNSON - GUILTY. Aged 18.
JONES - GUILTY. Aged 17.
SMITH GUILTY. Aged 15.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280410-253]
1044. JOHN LUTY was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of February, 1 mallet, value 2s., the goods of William Dance.
WILLIAM DANCE. I am a carpenter, and worked with the prisoner at an empty house in Holborn. On the 26th of February, I left my mallet there, and missed it in the morning; I found it in the room where the prisoner lives a fortnight after; he was apprehended by my master for robbing him, but the bill was thrown out - I lost some other tools about that time - another man worked there with us.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. Were you on the premises at the time you lost the mallet? A. No: after he was apprehended, his wife came there for some tools of his.
FRANCIS FAGAN. I apprehended the prisoner on the the 29th of February. Dance afterwards came to my house, and said he had lost a mallet; we went to the prisoner's room, and found a great many tools - Dance picked this mallet out.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280410-254]
1045. ANN NORRIS, MARIA DAVIS, and MARIA SMITH was indicted for stealing on the 8th of April, 1 pair of stays, value 5s., the goods of Sarah Moss.
SARAH MOSS. I am a stay-maker, and live in Prospect-row, Kingsland. On the 8th of April the three prisoners came together to my shop - Davis asked to look at a pair of stays, pointing to a pair marked 8s. 6d.; I showed them to her - Norris said, "You had better have coloured ones," and I took some coloured ones out of the window - Davis then said, "I had rather have cotton fronts" - I showed her some of them; they looked first at one pair, and then at another, and said they were too high a price; they took up the pair marked 8s. 6d., and Norris said, they were much the best, but I must take less for them - I offered them for 8s.; they said they could not affordthat - I said, they saw them ticketed, and knew the price before they came in - they began to turn round, and at that moment a balloon went up from Greenwich, which took our notice, and they bade me good afternoon - I directly missed a dark pair of stays - I followed, and found them walking arm-in-arm together, Norris in the middle - I called a neighbour and took hold of Norris. I said "You have got a pair of stays of mine" - she said "You wretch, you hussy, I have no such thing" - I said, "You have a dark pair of stays of mine, and I will not loose hold of you"- the officer came up; they were taken; and the stays found on Norris, she and Davis had been the acting parties - Smith took no share in the conversation.(Property produced and sworn to.)
JOHN BRIDE. I saw the prisoners taken back to Mrs. Moss' shop - I was standing by the right arm of Thompson, and heard Norris' clothes rustling - Thompson said,"there is something dropped;" I saw the stays on the floor and have no doubt that they fell from Norris.
MRS. MOSS. These are my stays.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. You saw them searched, did you not? A. Yes; six or seven shillings were found on them - there were no other persons in my shop when the balloon went up: I saw the stays drop - when they came in my counter was quite clear.
The prisoners all received a good character.
NORRIS - GUILTY. Aged 22.
DAVIS - GUILTY. Aged 20.
Confined Six Months.
SMITH - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280410-255]
1046. GEORGE HAWKINS was indicted for stealing, on the 31st of March, 1 handkerchief, value 6d.; 1 jacket, value 2s.; 1 waistcoat, value 3d.; 1 petticoat, value 1s.; 4 yards of cotton, value 2s.; and 1 shirt, value 1s; the goods of Elizabeth Webster.
ELIZABETH WEBSTER. I live at Islington, and am a widow. I keep a mangle: the prisoner is my nephew - he is a painter, but was out of work: I have not seen him for some time - I missed these articles about the 31st of March; I spoke to the prisoner, though I had no suspicion of him; he owned it directly, and said he did it though hunger.
JAMES TAYLOR. I am a constable. I took the prisoner at a coffee-shop; the person who keeps that shop produced this petticoat, which she said she had of the prisoner; he told me he sold this handkerchief for four-pence, in Steven-street; he took me to Mrs. Warley's, in the Harrow-road, where he said he had sold the jacket and waistcoat for one shilling.
MARIA WHITE. I keep a coffee-shop, at No. 31, Great James-street, Lisson Grove; the prisoner sold me this petticoat, an old shirt, and a bit of calico; he came to me at eight o'clock in the morning, and said he was straving: I said I had no money - he said he wanted no money if I would let him have a shilling's worth of victuals, and let him have the rest at night; I bought them of him for two shillings - they were not worth the money - the calico was not worth five-pence a yard when new.
CATHERINE WALLER. I live in Harrow-road, and keep a clothes shop, I bought an old jacket and a waistcoat of the prisoner for one shilling - the jacket was very old; I sold them in about three days for eighteen-pence.
CHARLES COTTLE. I am a constable: I went with Taylor: I have part of the petticoat, which I got when I went to the coffee-shop the second time.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Recommended to Mercy. - Fined 1s. and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18280410-256]
1047. JOHN CHENNER was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of April, 1 coat, value 30s.; 3 waistcoats, value 10s.; 2 handkerchiefs, value 10s.; two shirts, value 6s.: 1 pair of trousers, value 8s.: and 4 pairs of stockings, value 4s.; the goods of William Evans.
WILLIAM EVANS. I am a labourer; I had a bundle, containing all this property, and was going, on the 10th of April, to Cirencester, I was at Brentford trying to go by Tanner and Bailey's waggon - I gave the bundle to the prisoner, to carry it to the waggon - I had not known him before; I was to follow - but he ran faster than I could, to stop the waggon; it was about a half-past nine o'clock at night - I was following, and I met two men, who put me in the wrong road; I thought the prisoner went towards the waggon; I should have paid him if I could have found him.
JESSE FULLER. I am constable. About a half-past eleven o'clock at night I met the waggon near Brentford Bridge; the prosecutor came and told me he had been robbed, and a young man told me he had seen the prisoner running away from the waggon; I enquired and found the prosecutor had been drinking with him - he was a little the worse for liquor; I went to Old Brentford and found the prisoner in bed - I saw the woman whom he lives with at the door; I went up to the prisoner, and asked for the bag which he took from a pensioner; he said, "What bag" - I said, "If you don't produce it I shall take you out of the house;" I pressed him and he fetched it and gave it me - he was quite sober; I have known him a long time.
JOHN LAWLER. I am a gardener; about twenty minutes before eleven o'clock that night, I went into the Castle public-house, to have a pint of beer - I saw the prisoner, the prosecutor, and some others in the tap-room - they made some movement - the prisoner took the bundle, and ran out; the other man went to the prosecutor, and said, "Come, you must go, you can't lodge here tonight." They all rushed out, and the prisoner went quite in a contrary way to the waggon - I told the constable, and went with him to the prisoners.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. He put the bundle into my hand; that is all I have to say.
GUILTY. Aged 28.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280410-257]
1048. MARY CLIFFORD was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of April, 1 looking-glass, value 5s.; 1 bed, value 50s.; 1 bolster, value 5s.; 2 pillows, value 5s.; 3 blankets, value 9s.; 1 counterpane, value 4s.; and 2 pillow-cases, value 1s. 6d., the goods of Joseph Parr.
SARAH PARR. I am wife of Joseph Parr. We live in Meard's-court - I let two furnished rooms to the prisoner, on the 17th of March, at 5s. 6d. a week - she lived with me one week, and entered on another, when she leftsuddenly; leaving the door locked: after she was gone I went into her room - I missed all these things, and found the duplicate of this glass, between a card-table; I went to the pawnbroker's, to see if it was mine, and there I met her. All these things were let to her with the lodgings - I have since found the bed, which was pawned.
JAMES ALDIS. I am a pawnbroker. I have a looking-glass, pawned by the prisoner, on the 7th of April - I have known her for years.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was desired to pawn them by the person who took the apartments; I did not take them.
MRS. PARR re-examined. Q. Had she a husband? A. She said she had, but he never made his appearance; different men came to see her.
GUILTY. Aged 23.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280410-258]
1049. GEORGE BURLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of March, 1 copper boiler, value 12s., the goods of William Linnet.
WILLIAM HUBBARD. On the 6th of March, I saw the prisoner in Northampton-street, with a copper boiler, running from Mr. Linnet's house - I went and told him; he went in pursuit - I did not see him take the boiler.
WILLIAM LINNET. I live in Northampton-street, and am a broker. Hubbard told me, on the 6th of March, that the prisoner had gone off with a boiler - I pursued about 150 yards, and saw him carrying this boiler very leisurely along - I took him back - the boiler had been chained to the door - this is it.
GUILTY. Aged 14.
Recommended to mercy. - Confined Six Weeks.
[trialAccount t18280410-259]
1050. BARNET ABRAHAMS was indicted for stealing, on the 22d of March, 11 books, value 36s.; and one quilt, value 8s.; the goods of John Jones Price.
CHARLES BROWN. I am in the employ of Mr. Price, a pawnbroker of Wentworth-street. On the night of the 22d of March, about a quarter before eleven o'clock, I observed our inside window open; I was behind the counter opposite to it - I saw the prisoner with the window in one hand, and a bed quilt in the other, tied up in a handkerchief, which had been in the window; I gave an alarm - he went to the door and tried to escape; the shop was full of people - I went out and seized him; he dropped the quilt on his left side, the moment he got out at the door.
SUSANNAH SMITH. I was at Mr. Price's; I saw Brown jump over the counter, and go to the door; he took hold of the prisoner, and I took hold of him - I saw him drop the quilt and several books at the same time.
JOHN POWELL. I am a watchman; I took the prisoner down to the watch-house - I searched him, and found a pair of shoes on him.
JOHN DUNGATE. I produce the property which was brought to the watch-house with the prisoner.
JOHN JONES PRICE. I keep this shop; I was at home, and heard a bustle - I told Brown to jump over the counter, and see what was the matter, which he did; these articles are my property - they were brought in by different people out of the street; he came in by the door, and there are folding doors to the window, fastened with a button, eight feet high - I should not have thought a person could have reached it.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I went to pawn a pair of shoes that night; they accused me of having the property - but there was none on me, it was out at the door.
GUILTY. Aged 25.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280410-260]
1051. BENJAMIN SPENCER was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of March, 4 sows, price 16l., the property of Thomas Jackson.
THOMAS JACKSON. I live at Rose-well, Mile-end Old-town; I had four sows in a sty, in a cart-shed, which shuts with a pair of folding-gates - I locked them up at eight o'clock at night, on the 26th of March, and at five the next morning I missed them - I cannot say whether they had broken out of the sty, or whether it had been broken down; but the gates were wide open, and the lock had been forced off with a piece of iron, which I have in my pocket - I had had them about seven weeks, and fed them there regularly; they were all in pig, and I suppose about half their time gone, what farmers call, heavy in pig; I went round with my milk in the morning, and made enquiries, and in the afternoon a person said, I should find them in Green-street, Twig-folly - I left my milk, and went there; I saw Mr. Fernley, and gave him a description of them; he said it was perfectly right, and showed them to me directly - I knew them to be mine; his house is about three-quarters of a mile from mine - I have seen the prisoner about the neighbourhood carrying meat in a tray, as a hawking butcher.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Did you describe them as two white sows? A. Certainly; the other two were black and white; the prisoner lives in Devonshire-street, about three quarters of a mile from my place.
RICHARD FERNLEY. I keep a butcher's shop in Green-street, Twig-folly, about three quarters of a mile from the prosecutor. The prisoner used to pass my shop almost every day, and used to speak to me; on Wednesday night, the 26th of March, between half-past nine and ten o'clock at night, he came to me, and told me he had four pigs on the road, and he wished me to kill them for him the next day; I asked him where he brought them from - he said from Romford, and I supposed he had bought them there; the pigs came up directly - they were brought up by the prisoner and two other men; when they were put inside the slaughter-house he asked if I would kill two that night - I said No, it was too late, he must come in the morning; he had been with me several times before about pigs, and once that week he said he had offered money for some pigs, and could I make it convenient to kill them at my place, as he had no place to slaughter them himself; I had no conversation with the other men - they went away; I believe one of them came into the slaughter-house, but I do not think we had any conversation; they went away, and the prisoner remained for about ten minutes - while he was there we had a little light, and I said to him, "One of these sows is very heavy in pig" - he said, "Yes, I know it," when he looked at it, but he did not seem to know it before; I did not know the others were in pig that night - I was very busy, but when I had seen that one in pig I asked my wife if she thought it was all right, as there was not one of them in a fit state to be slaughtered; I went down to the prisoner about eleven o'clock that night - I found him in bed; I knocked at the door, and told him to come down; I asked him if the pigs were all right, as I did not think they were - he told me I had better let them loose in the street; he said he would be up in about half an hour, and he came to my house in that time, to say he would be down soon the next morning to fetch the pigs away - I had got an officer in the mean time, who took him when he came; the prisoner said he had offered money for the pigs, but he had altered his mind, and he thought he should not have them, and that they belonged to those two men - this was after eleven o'clock.
Cross-examined. Q. Whether this man received them improperly or not, there came two other men with him? A. Yes; they came between nine and ten o'clock; it had not struck ten when they came; my wife said afterwards that she heard the clock strike ten, but I did not - he said he brought them from Romford; I understood him to say he had brought them - the men brought the pigs in at the time he came; they came together.
RICHARD SKILLERN. I am a conductor of Bow-street. Between twelve and one o'clock at night I was fetched to Fernley's, and in about half an hour the prisoner knocked at the door; he went into the shop - I asked how he came by these pigs; he said he had bought them near the Globe public-house, in Mile-end-road, of a man who had brought them from Romford; that he knew one of the men, but could not recollect his name - I secured him; they were unfit to be killed.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you ask him to described the men? A. Yes, next morning; I have been in pursuit of them ever since - I know one to be a notorious thief; I have had him uicnstody before: I know nothing against the prisoner, but believe him to be a hard-working honest man; he has a wife and family; he told me the next morning that he offered 10l. for them and they wanted 12l.
Prisoner's Defence. I am quite innocent of knowing they were stolen.
JOHN PURCELL. I am a cow-keeper, and live in Essex-street, Globe-fields: I have known the prisoner about three years. On Wednesday evening, the 26th of March, I saw him about a quarter before nine o'clock, in his own house; I went out, and thought if he had a bit of beefsteak I would have it for supper; I looked through his window, and saw he had nothing but bits of mutton; I did not go in, but I saw him sitting there with a child on his knee. I had been into Whitechapel, on business; I know it was that Wednesday, because the next morning a gentleman called on me, and I told him I saw the man at home about nine o'clock.
COURT. Q. What makes you so particular as to time? A. Because it was eight o'clock when I went from my house to Whitechapel, and I looked at Whitechapel-church clock; it was Mr. Fernley called on me next morning; it would take me ten minutes to walk three quarters of a mile; I have driven a pig that distance in a quarter of an hour, but some are more obstinate than others.
RHODA CHITTAM. I am the wife of Thomas Chittam, a carpenter, who lives in West-street, next door to the prisoner; there is a yard attached to the prisoner's house, and I had taken some sheets there to dry; I went that evening at a quarter before nine o'clock, as near as I can guess, to get these sheets - he was at home, getting his supper, with his slippers on, and apparently going to bed; I wished him good night, and he shut the door after us.
COURT. Q. He is your neighbour, and you know him perfectly well? A. Yes; I have no doubt whatever that he was preparing to go to bed.
GUILTY. Aged 36.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280410-261]
1052. EDWARD JONES was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of February, 20lbs. weight of lead, value 3s., belonging to the Governors of the Hospital of the Poor , or Almshouse, called Sekford's Almshouse, in Woodbridge, in the County of Suffolk, and fixed to a certain building, situate in the Parish of St. James, Clerkenwell; belonging to the said Governors; against the Statute
THREE OTHER COUNTS varying the manner of desscribing the owners of the property.
MR. ALLEY conducted the prosecution.
PETER WARD. I am a labourer in the East India Company's service, and am employed by Mr. William Cook, an attorney, to take care of Woodbridge alms-house, Clerkenwell, for him. On the 28th of February, I went there about seven o'clock in the evening, and saw the prisoner in the court-yard with some lead lying at his feet; I caught hold of him, and he said "It is not me, a boy behind me has taken it;" I looked and saw another boy in his shirt sleeves; I secured the prisoner and the other got away - the prisoner then said there was another boy in the house, but in order to secure the prisoner, I took him to the next house; I then went back to the house - I found the bottom window open, but there was no one there; I went to the top of the house and found the attic window open: I afterwards went out and matched the lead which I had found in the court-yard, and it fitted exactly; there was about 20 lbs. weight of it - one boy could have carried it - the court-yard had been enclosed but the padlock had been forced off the gate; I had seen it only a night or two before.
GEORGE WADDINGTON. I am an officer. I produce the lead, which I have fitted on the top of the house - it matched exactly; the prisoner told me that he stood in the yard, and the other boys brought it out to him; I took the other two boys, but they were discharged for want of evidence.
SAMUEL STANILAND. I am clerk to the solicitor for the Trustees of this Hospital, under the Act of Parliament - Sir William Draper Best and Sir John Leach, are the Trustees.
Prisoner's Defence. I did not take it; I met the two boys whom I named to the officer - they took me there, brought out the lead, and laid it at my feet; the gentleman heard a noise - he came and took me, and the boys made their escape.
GUILTY. Aged 14.
There was another indictment against the prisoner.
Judgment Respited.
[trialAccount t18280410-262]
1053. JANE DIXON was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of April, 1 shawl, value 2s. 6d., the goods of James Martin Bass, from the person of Ruth Bass .
RUTH BASS. I am the wife of James Martin Bass. On the 11th of April, a little after eleven o'clock in the evening, I was with my husband going along Ratcliff-highway, my foot slipped, and I fell; a strange woman came up and offered to assist me, but she took my shawl off and ran away; I cannot say that it was the prisoner.
JAMES MARTIN BASS. I was behind my wife with the umbrella; she fell down, and I saw a woman come up to her - I cannot say it was the prisoner.
THOMAS CUNDALL. I am a watchman. I was coming along Shadwell; I saw several persons in the middle of the road; the prosecutor said "My wife has lost her shawl;" and some person said "There runs the woman" I took hold of the prisoner who was running with the shawl; I said "Give me that shawl;" I took her to the watch-house - she said "There is the shawl;" that was all that passed.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was not running; I was standing at the corner of Bluegate-field - a man came up and gave me the shawl, and asked me to take a walk with him - the watchman came up and I gave it to him.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18280410-263]
1054. SAMUEL VICKERY was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of April, 1 picture, value 40s. the goods of Thomas Serle.
THOMAS SERLE. I am a picture-dealer and live in Frith-street, Soho. I lost this picture on the 14th of April.
CHARLES HOPE. I am a straw hat-maker. The prosecutor has part of my house: between eight and nine o'clock in the morning of the 14th of April, the shop bell rang; the little boy who generally attends the door, went to it, and soon after called out "shop;" I went into the shop and missed a picture; I looked into the street and saw the prisoner with it, partly concealed under his arm, and just turning into a court; I pursued him, and called Stop thief! he ran pretty sharp, but there were three person on horseback who pursued him, and he was taken; I kept him in view all the time, except when he turned a corner; when I came up to him I asked where the picture was; he said, "I have no picture, this is what I have taken," and he gave me this card - I took him back the same way he had run, and the picture which he had dropped was given to me by of the neighbours. I knew the prisoner before.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. When I came back, the card was given to the gentleman by another person, not by me; the picture was given me by a man to hold, and when I had it he ran away. GUILTY. Aged 25.
Confined for Three Months.
[trialAccount t18280410-264]
1055. JOHN WILLIAMS was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of April, 40 lbs. weight of horse-hair, value 30s. the goods of Thomas Bird.
THOMAS FRANCIS. I am hackney coachman to Thomas Bird; he keeps his coaches in his yard, in Rathbone-place; on the 12th of April I was in Oxford-street, about a quarter before six o'clock in the morning; I saw the prisoner carrying this horse-hair, tied up with string; I called the watchman and he asked him where he got it, he said, "I got it out of that yard," alluding to my masters; I went to the yard and found the inside of two chariots and a coach all cut to pieces; he was quite a stranger.
THOMAS BIRD. I am a coach-master - my yard is in Rathbone-place; I was at home in bed. I had left my carriage all secure; when I came down that morning I found the linings cut, and the horse hair taken out, to the damage of more than 20l. - my yard is not locked at night, but the watchman minds it; I believe this to be the lining of the carriages.
JOHN FREEMAN. I am a watchman. I was called, and took the prisoner with this horse-hair.
Prisoner's Defence. When the watchman stopped me I told him that was the man who sent me for it, he was then about two yards before me; he took to his heels and ran off - several ran after him, but could not take him.
JOHN FREEMAN. I saw no other man.
THOMAS FRANCIS. There was a man several yards before him, but he did not run.
GUILTY. Aged 26.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280410-265]
1056. JAMES CUNNINGHAM & JOHN WYGOLD were indicted for stealing, on the 10th of April, 3 live tame fowls, price 6s., and 7 chickens, price 1s., the property of John Dixon.
JOHN DIXON. I live in Richardson-street, Stepney. On the 10th of April I got up between four and five o'clock in the morning; I saw my hen-roost open, which had been shut too - I went in, and missed a cock, two hens, and seven small chickens; I made enquiries, and found them all at Mr. Barber's, about a mile from my house - I had seen them all safe on the night before; the prisoner lived but a little way from me, and knew my place very well.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Was Cunningham ever in your place in his life? A. Not that I know of.
JOHN BARBER. I live at Bethnal-green. These two prisoners came to my house on Friday morning, about nine o'clock. Wygold had these fowls and chickens in a baker's basket; he said he had some fowls to sell; I asked what he wanted for them - he said 6s. 6d. or 7s. 6d; I said"I will give you 5s. 6d. for them," which I paid Wygold- they were very common fowls, middling size, and the chickens were but two or three days old.
JOHN DIXON. I think these fowls are mine, but fowls are very much alike; I should have sold them for 6s. or 7s.
CUNNINGHAM - NOT GUILTY.
WYGOLD - GUILTY. Aged 18.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Fourteen Days.

[trialAccount t18280410-267]
1058. GEORGE FOYLES was indicted for bigamy.
HENRY SMITH. I am clerk of the parish church of Cheadle, in Cheshire. I know the prisoner personally - he was married at Cheadle, in Cheshire, on the 16th ofMarch, 1824, to Mary Hibbert; I was present at the marriage, and saw it regularly performed; I made out their marriage register - I did not know her before; they had one child.
Prisoner. I never was married to such a person - he must allude to some other person.
Witness. I knew him before, and he knew me; he worked in Cheadle for the person who made shoes for me- I have not the least doubt he is the man.
Prisoner. Q. When did I live in Cheadle? A. In 1824, and worked there.
Prisoner. You are wrong, I never worked in Cheadle in my life; I only came from the East Indies in 1823 - if any woman can swear to me, let her come forward; who was the person who gave my wife away? Witness. I do not know, perhaps I might myself; I saw his wife alive three weeks ago.
SAMUEL VORLEY. I am an officer - I went to Cheadle, and produce a copy of the marriage register taken from the book, (read.)
ANN DEARY. I was married to the prisoner on the 19th of November, 1826, by the name of George Foyles, at St. James, Westminster; I had known him eight or nine months - I was at that time a widow; I had a house and let it furnished - I had a little boy: the prisoner represented himself to me as a shoemaker, and said his father kept the Jolly Hatters public-house, at Stockport, in Cheshire; I lived with him but six weeks, and then left him in consequence of his ill usage - he told me about a month after we were married that he had been married, and I then heard it from a young man who came from the country; I wrote to his wife who answered my letter, and then I would not live with him; he had pawned some of my things and I have worked very hard to get them out - I had to maintain him; he might do a little work, but what he got he spent; my former husband was a saddler, and we lived in Riley-street, Chelsea.
Prisoner. I never struck the woman in my life; but I could not bring home five or six guineas a week - and nothing would do; I gave her a pound or two when I could - she was jealous of another woman. Witness. It was not without reason; he cohabited with a married woman who lived close by.
Prisoner. I gave her all the money I could; I gave her my pension when I received it, and I said, "There is three pounds for you" - the day she left me, she robbed me of every thing. Witness. I left him furniture enough for a room; I took the other things away.
GUILTY. Aged 32.
Transported for Seven Years.
